1: # Build plan — Layer 2 (recursive) through Layer 3
2:

4: sections, which predate `worklist.py` / `pool.py` and are stale in specific,

9:

12: - **Part B — `main.py` + test mode**: the CLI that makes any of this runnable,
13:   and `--test-mode` for comparing model profiles on one segment.
14: - **Part C — Layer 3** (`generate_segment_dialogue.py`): the take function and

22: ## Part A — Layer 2 (recursive segmentation)
23: 
24: ### Context
25: 
26: The 3-layer generator (`utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/`) currently plans
27: each 6-hour segment with a **fixed** two-level fan-out: 2a always emits
28: exactly `chapters_per_segment` (9) chapters, 2b always emits ~19 slots per
29: chapter. That split exists purely because one segment's full slot list
30: (~170 entries, ~10.2k tokens) doesn't fit one LLM call — it isn't shaped
31: around where the story actually needs detail.
32: 
33: The proposal: make this scalable instead of fixed. A segment should be
34: plannable at whatever depth the content actually needs — a dense stretch (a
35: spine scene, a fork point, heavy state dependency) recurses further, a quiet
36: stretch (ambient downtime) stays shallow or is planned directly. This
37: generalizes the existing 2a/2b split from a hard-coded 2 levels into an
38: arbitrary-depth tree, driven by a deterministic word-budget check rather
39: than trusting a model's self-assessment of its own output density (matching
40: this codebase's existing philosophy — see `sensitivity_budget`,
41: `density_floor`, the fork convergence validator: guards are deterministic,
42: never trust-based).
43: 
44: Per the earlier discussion, **only the segment→...→slot portion becomes
45: recursive.** Layer 1 (`plan_arc.py`, arc→segment) stays exactly as built:
46: it's serial by nature (each batch needs the previous batch's
47: `continuity_out`), cheap (~20 min total), and already implemented/tested —
48: there's no efficiency to gain by touching it, and the story-density variance
49: being described lives inside a segment, not across segments.
50: 
51: #### Verified against the tree (2026-08-19)
52: 
53: - **`grep -rn "chapter" src/` outside Layer 2 returns nothing.** `config.py`,
54:   `worklist.py`, `pool.py`, `plan_arc.py`, `arc_schema.py`, `vocabulary.py`
55:   have zero coupling to the chapter concept. `config.py` does no schema
56:   validation of `segment:` keys at all, so adding a `tree:` block needs no
57:   loader change and no `test_config.py` change.
58: - **`worklist.py` touches only `brief["slots"]` and `slot["slot_id"]`.** Its
59:   one structural dependency is `take_path()` at `worklist.py:50`, which uses
60:   `slot_id` as a *directory name* (`segment_root / "slots" / slot_id`). This
61:   constrains the node-id scheme — see "Node ids" below.
62: - **There is no `main.py` yet** in the utility, despite `PLAN.md:86`
63:   describing one. There is therefore no caller of `plan_segment()` to update
64:   outside the tests.
65: - **`output/` holds nothing but `.gitkeep`** — no real run has happened, so
66:   there is no on-disk legacy `brief.yaml`/chapter data to migrate.
67: - **The two Layer 2 modules were generated by the qwen worker** from
68:   `tools/qwen_worker/specs/3layers_segment_schema.yaml` and
69:   `3layers_plan_segment.yaml`, whose goal prose hard-codes the 2a/2b design.
70:   Those specs are part of this change — see "Build workflow".
71: 
72: ### Decisions taken this session
73: 
74: | # | Decision | Rationale |
75: |---|---|---|
76: | 1 | Exhausted expand → **degrade the node to a forced leaf**, not drop it | Dropping a depth-1 node with weight 0.4 silently costs ~40% of a segment against today's ~11%. A forced leaf reuses the escape hatch `max_depth` needs anyway; content survives at coarser granularity. |
77: | 2 | **Continuous work queue**, not BFS level-by-level | A frontier containing one branch node idles 3 of 4 workers and gates everything behind it. Submitting children the moment a parent resolves keeps the pool busy; per-node checkpointing gives *better* crash safety than per-level (lose one node, not one level). |
78: | 3 | Prompts carry the **full ancestor summary chain** | At depth 1 the parent *was* `arc_segment`, so passing the node alone was complete. At depth 3 the intermediate summaries vanish and a leaf sees only the 6-hour synopsis plus its own one-liner — losing exactly the context the recursion exists to add. |
79: | 4 | **Rewrite the specs and regenerate via the qwen worker**, using `qwen3.8:27b` as the coding model | Same workflow that built these modules. `qwen3.8:27b` is pulled and available; `runner.py --model` already parameterizes it. |
80: 
81: ### Design
82: 
83: #### The node model (replaces the fixed chapter concept)
84: 
85: Every unit below the segment root — whether it ends up looking like today's
86: "chapter" or goes deeper — is the same record shape:
87: 
88: ```python
89: {
90:   "node_id": str,        # see "Node ids" below
91:   "parent_id": str | None,
92:   "order": int,           # 0-based, contiguous among siblings
93:   "depth": int,           # 0 = root (the segment itself)
94:   "title": str,
95:   "summary": str,
96:   "continuity_in": str,
97:   "continuity_out": str,
98:   "target_words": int,    # this node's share of airtime words
99:   "target_slots": int,    # derived: round(target_words / (words_per_take * takes_per_slot))
100:   "kind": "branch" | "leaf" | None,   # None = not yet resolved
101:   "forced": bool,         # True when max_depth or expand-failure forced the leaf
102:   "children": [node_id, ...],          # once kind == "branch"
103:   "slots": [...] | None,               # once kind == "leaf"
104: }
105: ```
106: 
107: The root node is `arc_segment` wrapped with `target_words =
108: config["segment"]["target_words"]` — everything else about `arc_segment`
109: (spine_scenes, synopsis, continuity) is passed through to every leaf's
110: prompt regardless of depth, exactly as `build_slot_prompt` already does
111: today (spine ground truth isn't chapter-scoped currently either).
112: 
113: ##### Node ids
114: 
115: `f"{parent_id}-n{order}"`, so root `seg-01` → child `seg-01-n2` →
116: grandchild `seg-01-n2-n0`. **Not** `{parent}.{order}`: `worklist.take_path`
117: (`worklist.py:50`) uses the prefixed `slot_id` as a literal directory name,
118: and dotted path components read as file extensions to every tool that later
119: globs that tree. Slot ids become `f"{node_id}-{slot_id}"` — the same
120: prefixing rule as today, just with a longer prefix.
121: 
122: #### The recursion trigger — deterministic, not model-trusted
123: 
124: A node is a **leaf** (planned directly into slots) once `target_slots <=
125: segment.tree.max_leaf_slots` (default 19, matching today's per-chapter
126: count). Otherwise it's a **branch**: one LLM call asks for child nodes, each
127: carrying a `weight` — its relative share of the parent's `target_words` —
128: instead of an equal split. This weight is the actual "scale up/down"
129: mechanism: a dense child (spine scene, high state-dependency) gets a bigger
130: weight → bigger `target_words` → likely still oversized after distribution
131: → recurses again. A quiet child gets a small weight → small `target_words`
132: → likely already leaf-eligible → no further calls.
133: 
134: Two things force a leaf regardless of size, both logged at WARNING and both
135: setting `forced: True`:
136: 
137: 1. **`max_depth` reached** (default 4).
138: 2. **Expand exhausted its retries** (decision 1). The node still gets a slot
139:    call for its full `target_slots` — past the size a single call
140:    comfortably covers, but it produces content rather than a hole.
141: 
142: A `min_node_words` floor (default 2000) is enforced by the validator on the
143: weight distribution, not requested of the model — a child whose
144: weight-derived word share falls under the floor fails validation and the
145: whole expand call is retried, the same retry-then-degrade pattern above.
146: 
147: #### `generation.yaml` — replace `chapters_per_segment` with a `tree:` block
148: 
149: ```yaml
150: segment:
151:   ...
152:   target_words: 53600
153:   target_slots: 170
154:   tree:
155:     max_leaf_slots: 19    # node this size or smaller is planned directly (today's ~19/chapter default)
156:     max_children: 12      # fan-out cap per expand call
157:     max_depth: 4          # safety cap below the segment root; hit + still oversized -> forced leaf, WARNING
158:     min_node_words: 2000  # anti-fragmentation floor, enforced by the validator
159:     leaf_density_floor: 0.80   # a leaf returning fewer than this * target_slots fails validation
160:   concurrency: 4
161:   max_attempts: 2
162:   sensitivity_budget: 0.40
163:   density_floor: 0.80
164: ```
165: 
166: A uniform-weight run (every child gets equal weight) reproduces today's
167: shape almost exactly — root splits into ~9 leaf children of ~19 slots each,
168: 1 expand + 9 leaf calls — so the default behavior doesn't regress; the new
169: behavior only appears when weights are lopsided.
170: 
171: **Knob interlock.** These four values can be set mutually infeasible (e.g.
172: `min_node_words` above the leaf threshold in words means every expand fails
173: validation, retries, degrades, and the run quietly produces one giant leaf
174: per segment). `validate_tree_config(config) -> List[str]`, called once at
175: the top of `plan_segment`, checks:
176: 
177: - `max_children >= 2`
178: - `min_node_words * 2 <= max_leaf_slots * words_per_take * takes_per_slot`
179:   (a branch node must be splittable into at least two floor-legal children)
180: - `max_depth >= 1`, `max_leaf_slots >= 1`, `0 < leaf_density_floor <= 1`
181: 
182: Problems raise `SegmentPlanError` — this is operator error, not model
183: error, and should fail loudly at startup rather than degrade silently.
184: 
185: #### `src/segment_schema.py` — generalize chapter/slot to node/expand/leaf
186: 
187: Renames and additions (pure functions, no I/O, same style as today):
188: 
189: - `SYSTEM_PROMPT_EXPAND` (was `SYSTEM_PROMPT_CHAPTERS`) — asks for child
190:   nodes with `{order, title, summary, continuity_in, continuity_out,
191:   weight}`, explains that weight reflects how much is happening in that
192:   slice and should scale with it.
193: - `SYSTEM_PROMPT_LEAF` (was `SYSTEM_PROMPT_SLOTS`) — unchanged in spirit;
194:   same ambient/spine slot shape as today.
195: - `parse_children` (was `parse_chapters`), `parse_slots` (unchanged).
196: - `leaf_eligible(target_slots, config) -> bool`.
197: - `derive_target_slots(target_words, config) -> int` — pulls
198:   `budget.measured_baseline.words_per_take` and `dialogue.takes_per_slot`,
199:   the same formula the top-level `target_slots` in generation.yaml already
200:   documents. Note the derived per-child slot counts are **not** guaranteed
201:   to sum to the parent's `target_slots` (independent rounding); words
202:   reconcile exactly, slots drift by at most one per child. `density_floor`
203:   absorbs this at the segment level and it is not worth reconciling twice.
204: - `distribute_words(children, node, config) -> children` — normalizes
205:   weights to sum 1, multiplies by `node["target_words"]`, rounds,
206:   reconciles the rounding remainder onto the largest child so the sum is
207:   exact (same "must sum exactly" discipline as fork `carry_out`
208:   elsewhere), and sets each child's derived `target_slots`.
209: - `validate_tree_config(config) -> List[str]` — the interlock check above.
210: - `validate_children(children, node, config) -> List[str]` (was
211:   `validate_chapters`) — required keys, contiguous 0..N-1 order, count
212:   within `[1, max_children]`, `weight` a positive number, non-empty
213:   `summary`, **non-empty `continuity_in`/`continuity_out` strings**, and
214:   every distributed child word share `>= min_node_words` (computed via
215:   `distribute_words` inside validation, so a floor violation is caught
216:   before any child node is created).
217: 
218:   *Deliberately not validated:* semantic continuity chaining (child 0's
219:   `continuity_in` actually matching the parent's). It is prose; there is no
220:   deterministic check for it, and this codebase does not add trust-based
221:   guards. Chaining is handled on the prompt side instead — siblings are
222:   generated in one call and see each other, and the ancestor chain (below)
223:   carries the parent's continuity down.
224: - `validate_slots(slots, pack, vocab, config, node=None)` — **correction to
225:   the earlier draft: today's `validate_slots` does not check slot count at
226:   all.** `target_slots` appears only in `build_slot_prompt` and the
227:   segment-level density check. Under recursion that gap widens (more leaves,
228:   more chances to under-deliver, one aggregate warning at the very end), so
229:   this adds one check: when `node` is given and
230:   `len(slots) < node["target_slots"] * leaf_density_floor`, that's a problem
231:   string → retry. Everything else about the function is unchanged.
232: - `build_expand_prompt(pack, arc_segment, ancestors, node, config, problems)`
233:   (was `build_chapter_prompt`) — renders the segment synopsis, then each
234:   ancestor's title + summary in depth order, then this node's
235:   synopsis/continuity/`target_words`, an expected-children guideline
236:   (`ceil(node.target_slots / max_leaf_slots)` capped at `max_children`), and
237:   asks for a `weight` per child.
238: - `build_leaf_prompt(pack, arc_segment, ancestors, node, config, problems)`
239:   (was `build_slot_prompt`) — same content as today's slot prompt plus the
240:   ancestor chain, slot count from `node["target_slots"]`.
241:   `ancestors` is `List[node]` root-exclusive, ordered shallow→deep; empty
242:   at depth 1, which makes these prompts byte-comparable to today's for a
243:   uniform run.
244: - `merge_brief(arc_segment, tree, config) -> Dict` (was chapter-keyed) —
245:   DFS-walks the tree in `order` (preserves the "drain in arc order"
246:   property), flattens every leaf's slots into the same
247:   `{segment_id, order, loop, hours, synopsis, continuity_in,
248:   continuity_out, carry_in, carry_out, slots: [...]}` shape `worklist.py`
249:   already reads. Each slot's provenance tag becomes `node_id` (replacing
250:   `chapter_id`). Adds an optional `nodes:` summary (id, depth, kind,
251:   forced, target_slots, actual slot count) so a human reading `brief.yaml`
252:   by hand at Stage 2 can see the tree shape a run actually produced.
253: - `check_sensitivity_budget` — unchanged, but see the call-site change below.
254: 
255: #### `src/plan_segment.py` — a continuous work queue over the tree
256: 
257: 1. **Startup**: `validate_tree_config` → raise on operator error.
258: 2. **Resume**: load `tree.yaml` if present; else seed the root node from
259:    `arc_segment`. Path is derived, **not** a new argument —
260:    `out_path.with_name("tree.yaml")` — so `plan_segment`'s signature is
261:    unchanged and no caller or test fixture has to grow a parameter.
262: 3. **Drain**: one `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=
263:    config["segment"]["concurrency"])` for the whole run. Seed it with every
264:    unresolved node; as each future completes, classify and submit
265:    immediately rather than waiting for a level to finish:
266:    - `depth >= max_depth` → forced leaf (WARNING, `forced: True`)
267:    - `leaf_eligible(...)` → leaf
268:    - otherwise → branch
269:    - **branch task**: `build_expand_prompt` → LLM → `parse_children` →
270:      `validate_children` → `distribute_words`, retried to `max_attempts`.
271:      Exhausted → WARNING, reclassify this node as a forced leaf and
272:      resubmit it as a leaf task (decision 1).
273:    - **leaf task**: `build_leaf_prompt` → LLM → `parse_slots` →
274:      `validate_slots` (now including the per-leaf density check), retried to
275:      `max_attempts`. Slot ids prefixed with `node_id`. Exhausted → ERROR +
276:      empty `slots` for that node; this is the only remaining skip path and
277:      it costs at most one leaf's worth of slots, same as today.
278:    - Because a branch task enqueues new work, the drain is a
279:      `while pending:` loop over `as_completed`-style bookkeeping rather than
280:      a single `with executor:` block — new futures must be admissible after
281:      the first wave.
282: 4. **Checkpoint after every completed node**: write `tree.yaml`, recompute
283:    and write `brief.yaml` via `merge_brief`. A crash loses at most one
284:    node — strictly better than today's "at most one chapter", and better
285:    than the level-batched alternative.
286: 5. **Final**:
287:    - `check_sensitivity_budget` on the **flattened segment slot list**, not
288:      per leaf. Per-leaf was equivalent to per-chapter (~19 slots) before;
289:      under a tree a 6-slot leaf with 2 flag-sensitive slots reads as 33% and
290:      warns on noise. The segment-level figure is the one the budget was
291:      written about (`generation.yaml`: "max fraction of a segment's slots").
292:      Per-leaf remains as a DEBUG line only.
293:    - Same segment `density_floor` check as today (flattened slot count vs.
294:      `target_slots * density_floor`).
295:    - Tree-shape summary log line: nodes total, leaves, forced leaves,
296:      depth range, slots, nodes skipped.
297: 
298: ### Merged from PLAN.md — Layer 2 items the implementation dropped
299: 
300: PLAN.md's Layer 2 pseudocode (`PLAN.md:311-382`) specifies more than
301: `segment_schema.py` shipped. These are folded into the prompt-builder and
302: guard rewrites rather than left as a second pass.
303: 
304: **M1 — The parallelism precondition, generalized (`PLAN.md:337-343`).** This
305: is the invariant that keeps all 28 segments concurrent, and the tree must not
306: break it:
307: 
308: > Cross-segment continuity flows through `arc_plan.yaml` — which Layer 1 has
309: > already written — and *never* through a sibling segment's `brief.yaml`.
310: 
311: Generalized to the tree: **a node's context comes from its ancestor chain and
312: from the single expand call that created it and its siblings — never from a
313: cousin's resolved slots, and never from another segment.** Siblings agree
314: because one call wrote all of them; cousins never need to agree, because both
315: descend from summaries that were already reconciled higher up. This is what
316: makes decision 2's continuous work queue sound — any two unresolved nodes can
317: run at the same instant regardless of position. Restate it verbatim in the
318: rewritten spec files; it is the one property whose violation collapses Layer 2
319: into a serial chain without failing a single test.
320: 
321: **M2 — Prompt content the shipped `build_slot_prompt` dropped.** PLAN.md
322: specs the slot prompt as synopsis + continuity + **relevant lore** (each
323: `spine_scenes` entry's `pack.scene(id).lore`, plus `ambient_focus` scenes'
324: lore) + cast roster + each spine scene's **actual `enter_narration` and beat
325: text** + an explicit `target_words` / `target_slots` stop condition. The
326: shipped builder (`segment_schema.py:379`) has only: chapter summary, slot
327: count, state flags, cast, spine `enter_narration`.
328: 
329: The lore omission is issue #3's live symptom and the cheapest to fix.
330: `vocabulary.unknown_lore` (`vocabulary.py:74`) validates every declared stem
331: against `pack.lore` and its error message even names the legal set — but the
332: prompt never shows the model that set, so a leaf call is a guess-and-retry on
333: lore by construction. `build_leaf_prompt` must render the legal stems the
334: same way it already renders legal state keys.
335: 
336: Add to `build_leaf_prompt`, all of it already reachable from `pack` and the
337: node:
338: 
339: - legal lore stems (`sorted(pack.lore.keys())`), presented as a closed set
340: - for each `arc_segment["spine_scenes"]` id: `enter_narration` **and** beat
341:   text, not just the narration line
342: - `ambient_focus` scenes' lore, where the arc segment declares them
343: - the node's `target_words` alongside `target_slots`, as an explicit stop
344:   condition — PLAN.md is specific that a concrete count beats an open-ended
345:   "sequence of beats", and under the tree that count is per-node rather than
346:   per-segment
347: 
348: **M3 — Event roll points (`PLAN.md:377`, D15).** "Mark each chapter boundary
349: as an event roll point" was never implemented and chapters are now gone. Per
350: the D19 decision, roll points become **leaf boundaries**: `merge_brief` emits
351: `roll_points: [node_id, ...]` in DFS order alongside `nodes:`, and event
352: seeds derive from `(events.seed, run_id, node_id)` instead of `chapter_id`.
353: Layer 2 only *emits* the list — nothing consumes it until `src/events.py`
354: exists, and that is out of scope here. Consequence to record in D15: leaf
355: sizes vary, so "~252 rolls over 168 h" stops being arithmetic and becomes an
356: estimate bounded by `max_leaf_slots`.
357: 
358: **M4 — The density guard should measure words, not slot count.** N5's
359: post-L2 guard is "segment slots sum to within ~80% of `target_words`"; the
360: shipped check compares `len(brief["slots"])` against `target_slots *
361: density_floor`. Those agreed when every slot was one ninth of a uniform
362: chapter. Under variable-size nodes they don't, and the word figure is now
363: directly computable — every node carries `target_words`. Keep the slot-count
364: check (it is what catches a leaf returning nothing) and **add** the word
365: check: `sum(leaf.target_words for leaf in leaves)` against
366: `segment.target_words * density_floor`, reported in the same WARNING.
367: 
368: **M5 — Sensitivity budget: "mark the segment for re-brief" (`PLAN.md:371`).**
369: The shipped code only logs a WARNING; nothing records that the segment should
370: be re-briefed, so a 168-hour run's over-budget segments are discoverable only
371: by grepping logs. `merge_brief` writes `needs_rebrief: true` into
372: `brief.yaml` when the flattened segment mix exceeds `sensitivity_budget`, and
373: the Part B CLI grows `--rebrief` to re-plan exactly those segments. Cheap,
374: and it makes the guard actionable instead of advisory.
375: 
376: **M6 — Intermediate artifacts.** PLAN.md specs `chapters.yaml` plus
377: `chapters/<chapter_id>/slots.yaml` per chapter, merged into `brief.yaml` once
378: complete. `tree.yaml` replaces both: it holds the node records *and* each
379: leaf's slots, so it is the single working checkpoint and `brief.yaml` is
380: purely derived. Per-node slot files would multiply with depth for no gain now
381: that resume reads one file.
382: 
383: ### Build workflow (decision 4)
384: 
385: The qwen worker writes only `target_files` and reads `test_files` as
386: context (`prompting.py:72`, `prompting.py:97`). **The tests must therefore
387: exist before the worker runs.** Order:
388: 
389: 1. Hand-write `config/generation.yaml`'s `tree:` block.
390: 2. Hand-write `tests/segment_helpers.py`, `tests/test_segment_schema.py`,
391:    `tests/test_plan_segment.py` against the design above.
392: 3. Rewrite `tools/qwen_worker/specs/3layers_segment_schema.yaml` and
393:    `3layers_plan_segment.yaml` — goal prose, the node model, the work-queue
394:    contract, the forced-leaf degradation rule.
395: 4. `python tools/qwen_worker/runner.py --model qwen3.8:27b preflight`
396: 5. `python tools/qwen_worker/runner.py --model qwen3.8:27b run
397:    tools/qwen_worker/specs/3layers_segment_schema.yaml` → review → `promote`
398: 6. Same for `3layers_plan_segment.yaml` (it imports the schema module, so it
399:    must run second).
400: 7. Change `ollama_client.DEFAULT_MODEL` to `qwen3.8:27b` if it should be the
401:    standing default rather than a per-invocation flag.
402: 
403: `qwen3.8:27b` is pulled and available locally. `runner.py --model` already
404: parameterizes it (`runner.py:242`); `DEFAULT_NUM_CTX` is 32768 and these two
405: specs plus their test files are the largest prompts in the spec set — if the
406: worker truncates, raise `--num-ctx` before blaming the model.
407: 
408: ### Tests
409: 
410: - `tests/segment_helpers.py`: `segment_config` fixture gains `tree:`, drops
411:   `chapters_per_segment`; `chapter()` builder becomes
412:   `child_node(order, weight=1.0, **overrides)`; `FakeSegmentLLM` dispatches
413:   on `SYSTEM_PROMPT_EXPAND`/`SYSTEM_PROMPT_LEAF` and defaults to a
414:   uniform-weight 9-child reply so most existing test bodies port with only
415:   naming changes.
416: - `tests/test_segment_schema.py`: rename chapter/slot-prompt tests to
417:   node/expand/leaf; **add** tests for `leaf_eligible`'s threshold,
418:   `distribute_words` (normalization, exact-sum rounding reconciliation,
419:   floor rejection), `validate_children`'s weight-floor and non-empty
420:   continuity checks, `validate_tree_config`'s interlock rejections, the new
421:   per-leaf density check in `validate_slots`, a lopsided child that still
422:   exceeds `max_leaf_slots` after distribution, and that `build_leaf_prompt`
423:   renders every ancestor summary at depth 3.
424: - `tests/test_plan_segment.py`: rework the "one chapter call / one slot call
425:   per chapter" tests into "one expand call at the root, further expand calls
426:   only for still-oversized children, leaf calls for right-sized ones".
427:   **Add**: (a) a lopsided-weight expand reply produces asymmetric tree depth
428:   — the heavy child recurses, the light ones don't; (b) `max_depth`
429:   forced-leaf safety valve; (c) **an expand that fails every attempt yields a
430:   forced leaf with slots, not an empty subtree** (decision 1 — this is the
431:   behavior most worth pinning); (d) resume from a partial `tree.yaml`
432:   mid-drain; (e) a branch resolving mid-run enqueues its children into the
433:   same pool without waiting for its siblings (decision 2). Crash-safety,
434:   concurrency and density-floor tests port over with renamed fixtures.
435: - `tests/test_worklist.py` and everything under Layer 1
436:   (`test_plan_arc.py`, `test_arc_schema.py`) — **not touched**, and must
437:   still pass unmodified as the check that the `brief.yaml` contract didn't
438:   drift.
439: 
440: 
441: ## Part B — `main.py` and test mode
442: 
443: Nothing in the utility is runnable today: `main.py` does not exist, so Layer 1
444: and Layer 2 are reachable only from tests. This is the smallest piece of work
445: in the doc and it gates the value of everything else.
446: 
447: Per CLAUDE.md, `main.py` is a thin orchestrator — argparse, calls into `src/`,
448: minimal logic of its own. Every `src/` module already exposes a plain function
449: for it to call and none carry their own argparse.
450: 
451: ```
452: main.py --stage {arc|segment|dialogue|all}
453:         [--pack PATH] [--config PATH] [--segments ID[,ID...]]
454:         [--model-profile NAME] [--test-mode] [--rebrief]
455:         [--dry-run] [--log-level LEVEL]
456: ```
457: 
458: - `--stage arc` → `plan_arc.plan_arc(...)`, writes `arc_plan.yaml`.
459: - `--stage segment` → for each segment id, `plan_segment.plan_segment(...)`.
460:   Default segment set is every segment in `arc_plan.yaml` without a
461:   `brief.yaml`; `--rebrief` selects those whose brief carries `needs_rebrief`
462:   (M5) and re-plans them from scratch.
463: - `--stage dialogue` → Part C.
464: - `--stage all` → arc → segment → dialogue, resumable at each layer.
465:   `config.validate_profile_for_stages` (`config.py:124`) already exists for
466:   the `--model-profile` + `--stage all` interaction and issue #10 wants that
467:   documented, not re-solved — call it and surface its error.
468: - `--dry-run` on the segment stage prints the tree that *would* be planned
469:   from the config's budget arithmetic (root `target_slots`, expected children,
470:   expected depth) without an LLM call. Cheap, and the fastest way to sanity
471:   check a `tree:` block after changing a knob.
472: 
473: ### Test mode (`PLAN.md:231`)
474: 
475: `--test-mode` requires `--segments <one-id>` (to keep it cheap) and runs
476: **every** profile under the active layer's `models:` map rather than just
477: `active_model`. Output goes to `<output.dir>/<pack>/_test/<stage>/<profile>/`
478: — separate and non-resumable, so it can never pollute a real `arc_plan.yaml`,
479: `brief.yaml`, `tree.yaml` or manifest. After each profile it prints one line:
480: model, wall-clock seconds, word/slot count.
481: 
482: The mechanism already exists — `config.resolve_profile(config, layer,
483: profile_name)` returns a flat dict and `concurrent_llm.from_profile` builds a
484: client from it, so test mode is a loop calling the same layer function once
485: per name from `config.profile_names(layer)`. No layer function changes.
486: 
487: This is the practical answer to "is `qwen3.8:27b` good enough to plan with, or
488: does Layer 2 need `hermes3:70b`" — the same question decision 4 raises for the
489: *coding* model, asked about the *planning* model, and answerable in one
490: segment's GPU time instead of by argument.
491: 
492: ## Part C — Layer 3 (`src/generate_segment_dialogue.py`)
493: 
494: ### Where PLAN.md is stale
495: 
496: `PLAN.md:384-424` was written before `worklist.py` and `pool.py` existed and
497: describes work those modules now own. Building it as written would duplicate
498: them and reintroduce the race D8 exists to prevent. Corrections:
499: 
500: | PLAN.md says | Actually |
501: |---|---|
502: | `for each slot in brief.scenes` | The key is **`slots`**. `worklist.build_worklist` does `brief["slots"]`; the wrong key yields an empty worklist and a run that reports success in three seconds having generated nothing. |
503: | "plan this slot's take conditions" inline | `worklist.conditions_for` (`worklist.py:55`) already implements D12's full rule — neutral takes, `sensitivity: none`, the tone/flags candidate list, the modulo. Do not reimplement. |
504: | "resume at next unused take number under `scenes/<slot_id>/`" | `worklist.take_path` writes `slots/<slot_id>/NNN.yaml`. Resume **is** `build_worklist` — re-running the scan is the resume (D8), and `_next_take_number` is exactly the racy call D8 replaced. |
505: | serial `for takes_per_slot:` loop | `pool.run_pool` drains it: per-worker improviser, one shared client, single writer thread, circuit breaker. |
506: | "imports `_generate_take` straight from `campaign.batch_generate` — same primitive, zero duplication" | **Contradicted by resolved issue #6 and D8.** `_generate_take` calls `improviser.update_context(scene=scene, loop=take, carry={})` (`batch_generate.py:167`) — it overwrites `loop` with the take number and blanks `carry` unconditionally, which is the only channel the arc's time-loop state has. Layer 3 needs its own ~10-line take function. `_write_take`, `_append_manifest` and `_beat_to_dict` reuse stays intact. |
507: 
508: PLAN.md's Layer 3 section should be corrected in place rather than left to
509: contradict its own issue log.
510: 
511: ### The local take function
512: 
513: ```python
514: def generate_take(improviser, unit, config):
515:     """One take for one WorkUnit. Never raises; [] is a failed attempt."""
516:     scene = Scene(id=unit.slot_id, prompt=unit.slot["prompt"],
517:                   lore=unit.slot.get("lore", []))
518:     improviser.recent = []
519:     improviser.update_context(scene=scene,
520:                               loop=unit.slot.get("loop", 1),
521:                               carry=unit.conditions)
522:     beats = improviser.generate_scene(scene)
523:     return [_beat_to_dict(b) for b in beats]
524: ```
525: 
526: Two things this pins down that nothing currently states:
527: 
528: - **`loop` comes from the brief, not the take number** — issue #6's fix. The
529:   segment's `loop` is already on `brief["loop"]` and `merge_brief` carries it;
530:   thread it onto each slot (or read it from the brief in the closure).
531: - **`WorkUnit.conditions` reaches the model through `carry`.**
532:   `update_context(scene, carry, loop)` (`improviser.py:29`) is the *only*
533:   context channel, and `generate_scene` renders `Loop N` plus each carry
534:   key/value into the user prompt (`improviser.py:210-213`). So
535:   `{"helen-wounded": True}` and `{"mood": "tense"}` both ride the carry
536:   channel and render as key/value lines. Nothing in PLAN.md said how a
537:   computed condition became prompt text; this is the answer, and it is worth
538:   a test — `conditions_for` producing a mood dict that never reaches the
539:   prompt would leave every conditioned take silently identical to its neutral
540:   sibling.
541: 
542: Retries are the pool's job (`max_attempts`), not this function's — hence no
543: attempt loop, unlike `_generate_take`.
544: 
545: ### Wiring
546: 
547: ```
548: load pack, config; resolve the dialogue profile -> one shared PooledOllamaClient
549: segment_ids = args.segments or every segment with a brief.yaml
550: units = worklist.build_worklist(output_root, segment_ids, config)   # the resume
551: log the estimate: worklist.estimate_seconds(units, seconds_per_take, concurrency)
552: stats = pool.run_pool(
553:     units,
554:     worker_factory=lambda: LLMImproviser(pack, shared_client, ...),  # ONE PER THREAD
555:     generate=lambda worker, unit: generate_take(worker, unit, config),
556:     writer=lambda unit, beats: (_write_take(...), _append_manifest(...)),
557:     concurrency=config["dialogue"]["concurrency"],
558:     max_attempts=config["dialogue"]["max_attempts"],
559:     breaker=CircuitBreaker(**config["dialogue"]["breaker"]))
560: guard: every ambient slot has exactly one take with conditions {}
561: report: takes written, words, per segment; neutral-take coverage; failures
562: ```
563: 
564: - **Spine slots are never generated.** `kind: spine` is scripted canon;
565:   `units_for_segment` should already exclude them — confirm with a test rather
566:   than assuming, since a spine slot reaching the pool would overwrite authored
567:   content.
568: - **The neutral-take guard is hard (issue #14).** Every ambient slot must end
569:   with exactly one take whose `conditions` is `{}`. A run finishing without it
570:   has produced a library that can dead-air, which is the one failure the whole
571:   `neutral_takes` design exists to prevent. Failing the guard is a non-zero
572:   exit, not a warning.
573: - **The stinger pass is out of scope.** It depends on `events.yaml`, which
574:   N2b has not produced. Leave the hook (`--stingers` rejected with a clear
575:   "requires events.yaml, not yet built") rather than a stub that silently
576:   does nothing.
577: 
578: ### `generation.yaml` — the circuit breaker has no config block
579: 
580: `pool.CircuitBreaker(window, failure_rate, min_samples)` (`pool.py:53`) takes
581: three parameters and **none of them appear in `generation.yaml`**. A
582: multi-day unattended run is exactly where a breaker matters (PLAN.md:649: a
583: dead ollama produces empty takes forever without raising), so it needs real,
584: reviewable values rather than constants buried in `main.py`:
585: 
586: ```yaml
587: dialogue:
588:   ...
589:   breaker:
590:     window: 50           # rolling sample size
591:     failure_rate: 0.5    # trip above this fraction failing
592:     min_samples: 20      # never trip before this many samples
593: ```
594: 
595: ### Tests — `tests/test_generate_segment_dialogue.py`
596: 
597: - Reads a fake `brief.yaml`; spine slots produce no work units, ambient slots
598:   do.
599: - `generate_take` calls the patched `improviser.generate_scene`, **not**
600:   `batch_generate._generate_take`.
601: - The segment's `loop` from the brief reaches `update_context` — issue #6's
602:   "done when", covered by a test.
603: - A `WorkUnit` with `conditions={"mood": "tense"}` renders that condition into
604:   the prompt `generate_scene` receives (the carry-channel mapping above).
605: - The neutral-take guard fails a library missing a `conditions: {}` take and
606:   passes a complete one.
607: - No network: a fake client, per D8's one-improviser-per-worker contract
608:   already covered by `test_pool.py`.
609: 
610: ## Part D — cross-cutting (issue #11)
611: 
612: `docs/` is empty and nothing in the utility pushes to Loki. Per the answer
613: this session, scope is **new and changed functions only** — the backlog for
614: the other seven modules stays filed as issue #11.

626:   long unattended run where log quality *is* the observability story: TRACE on
627:   entry/exit, DEBUG at each branch/leaf classification and around every LLM
628:   call, INFO on segment completion, ERROR inside every handler. The existing
629:   modules are already close; the new tree code should not regress it.

631:   each major operation:
632:   - `{app: "three_layers", operation: "plan_segment"}` — per segment: node
633:     count, leaf count, forced leaves, max depth, slots, skipped, wall-clock.
634:   - `{app: "three_layers", operation: "generate_dialogue"}` — per segment:
635:     takes written, words, failures, neutral-take coverage.
636:   - ERROR level from the handlers that currently only `log.error`.
637:   The push never raises and returns `False` when Loki is down, so it needs no
638:   error handling of its own.

676: ## Build order
677: 
678: Parts are sequenced so each is verifiable before the next depends on it.
679: 
680: 1. **A** — Layer 2 tree: config block, tests, specs, qwen regeneration
681:    (`--model qwen3.8:27b`), promote. `pytest -q` green.
682: 2. **B** — `main.py` + test mode. First point at which anything is runnable.
683: 3. **Stage 1-2 by hand**: run `--stage arc` for real, read `arc_plan.yaml`;
684:    then `--stage segment --test-mode --segments <one>` to pick the planning
685:    model, then brief two segments and read them. PLAN.md is emphatic that
686:    `arc_plan.yaml` is the artifact everything else depends on and is read
687:    before more GPU time is spent — the tree design does not change that.
688: 4. **N1 concurrency benchmark** (~20 min GPU, from the design-decisions doc):
689:    hermes3:70b at parallel 1/4/8/12 with the real Layer 3 prompt shape,
690:    recording aggregate words/min. `dialogue.concurrency` and `num_ctx` come
691:    from the knee of that curve, and Part C's pool is sized by it. Do this
692:    before writing Layer 3, not after.
693: 5. **C** — Layer 3, verified on the two segments from step 3, confirming the
694:    manifest and resume behave under concurrency, before any full run.
695: 6. **D** — docs, log levels, Loki, alongside each part rather than after all
696:    of them.

**Output:**
```
<path>/home/secus/codeProjects/virtualTubers/README.md</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # virtualTubers
2: 
3: ## Summary
4: 
5: virtualTubers is an autonomous AI-powered VTuber streaming system where a team of AI agents (Manager, Coder, Tester) act as a live software development team. Each agent runs in its own Docker container, has its own personality and ASCII-art avatar, works inside a live terminal session (tmux + neovim/htop/etc.), and streams that session to Twitch over RTMP via ffmpeg. It's for anyone who wants to run an always-on, config-driven "AI dev team" stream without hand-building the streaming pipeline from scratch.
6: 
7: The project is early-stage but the core loops are real: the agent brain (`app/agent.py`) has a perceive/think/act slice — it publishes heartbeats every tick and dispatches every incoming message type through role-gated handlers backed by a provider-switchable LLM (Ollama or Claude): the coder narrates a task and hands the commit to the tester, the tester reports `test_passed`/`bug_report` to the manager, and the manager re-delegates fixes (bounded at 3 retries) or reports back to the operator. Coders write real code through swappable backends (native / OpenCode / aider) and the tester really runs pytest against their workspaces. On top of that sits **Rerun Theater**: past real Claude Code dev sessions replay as paced, redacted shows — now with per-airing, two-voice **spoken narration** (boss + coder via local TTS) synchronized to the on-screen action. The terminal avatar (`app/avatar.py`) is still a simple expression-cycling stub. See the Phase 1 roadmap in the architecture doc for what's next.
8: 
9: See [docs/VTuber_AI_Dev_Team_Concept.md](docs/VTuber_AI_Dev_Team_Concept.md) for the full architecture and design plan.
10: 
11: ## Changelog
12: 
13: Dated write-ups of every feature and fix live in **[CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md)**
14: (newest first) — moved out of this file so the README stays a quick
15: orientation rather than a running history. Latest entry: the Rerun Theater
16: episode library moved from the filesystem into Postgres, gated behind a
17: shape/name/leak-audit/dry-run-render validator on upload.
18: 
19: ## Prerequisites
20: 
21: - Docker and Docker Compose
22: - An RTMP destination — a Twitch stream key for live streaming, or a local RTMP preview server (bundled via `rtmp-preview` in `docker-compose.yml`) for local testing
23: - (Optional) A running [Ollama](https://ollama.ai) instance for local LLM inference — the default worker config points at `http://localhost:11434`
24: - (Optional) An [Anthropic API key](https://console.anthropic.com/) if any worker's config sets `llm.provider: claude` instead of `ollama`
25: - (Optional) Piper voice models for spoken replay narration — fetched with `scripts/download_voices.py`, see [Rerun Theater](docs/usage.md#rerun-theater--replaying-past-sessions-with-voices)
26: - A reachable Kafka broker (agents/services publish and consume inter-agent messages there) and a Postgres instance (every message is durably logged there) — neither is bundled in `docker-compose.yml`; point at existing instances via `.env`
27: 
28: ## Installation
29: 
30: 1. Clone the repository:
31:    ```bash
32:    git clone <repo-url>
33:    cd virtualTubers
34:    ```
35: 2. Build the worker image (the `docker-compose.yml` expects a locally-built image and never pulls):
36:    ```bash
37:    docker build -t vtube-worker:latest .
38:    ```
39: 3. Copy `.env.example` to `.env` and fill in your stream keys, Kafka bootstrap servers, and Postgres credentials:
40:    ```bash
41:    cp .env.example .env
42:    ```
43:    ```bash
44:    CODER_STREAM_KEY=your_twitch_stream_key
45:    MANAGER_STREAM_KEY=your_twitch_stream_key
46:    TESTER_STREAM_KEY=your_twitch_stream_key
47:    STREAM_RTMP_URL=rtmp://live.twitch.tv/app   # omit to use the local rtmp-preview server
48: 
49:    KAFKA_BOOTSTRAP_SERVERS=your_kafka_host:9092
50:    KAFKA_TOPIC=vtuber.messages
51: 
52:    POSTGRES_HOST=your_postgres_host
53:    POSTGRES_PORT=5432
54:    POSTGRES_DB=your_db
55:    POSTGRES_USER=your_user
56:    POSTGRES_PASSWORD=your_password
57:    ```
58:    `.env` is gitignored — never commit real credentials.
59: 
60: ## Git Remotes & GitHub Mirror
61: 
62: This repo pushes to a homelab Gitea instance (`origin`), which auto-mirrors
63: every push to GitHub (`github`, read-only) within seconds. Full remote URLs,
64: the mirror credential's expiry date, and health-check commands are in
65: **[docs/git_remotes.md](docs/git_remotes.md)**.
66: 
67: ## Usage
68: 
69: Start the full stack (three workers + message-logger + message-api + Redis + local RTMP preview):
70: 
71: ```bash
72: docker compose up
73: ```
74: 
75: This launches three worker containers — `worker-coder`, `worker-manager`, `worker-tester` — plus `message-logger`, `message-api`, a shared `redis` instance, and an `rtmp-preview` server for local testing. Each worker boots a virtual display and tmux session, starts the agent loop (narrating work as it flows coder → tester → manager → operator over the Kafka bus), and streams that session out over RTMP.
76: 
77: To preview locally without a real Twitch key, leave `STREAM_RTMP_URL` unset (it defaults to `rtmp://rtmp-preview:1935/live`) and view the stream with a player like VLC pointed at `rtmp://localhost:1935/live/<stream_key>`.
78: 
79: Send a worker an instruction via `message-api` (port `8090`):
80: 
81: ```bash
82: curl -X POST http://localhost:8090/messages \
83:   -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
84:   -d '{"to": "coder", "type": "task_assignment", "payload": {"task": "say hello"}}'
85: ```
86: 
87: Or drive the same controls — worker on/off, log filtering, message
88: injection, log pruning, the Rerun Theater replay library — from a browser at
89: **http://localhost:8091** (`control-panel`, docs/control_panel.md), no curl
90: required.
91: 
92: For everything else — shelling into a container, the full inter-agent
93: messaging protocol, pausing/resuming a worker, running Rerun Theater (solo
94: shows and multi-worker duets, with spoken narration), and local development
95: outside Docker — see **[docs/usage.md](docs/usage.md)**.
96: 
97: ## Deployment (Docker Compose on d2000)
98: 
99: The stack runs on **d2000**, a Windows machine on the local network running
100: Docker Desktop, via plain `docker compose` — there is no Portainer in front
101: of it. **The worker image is never built by `docker compose up`** — the
102: three workers use `image: vtube-worker:latest` with `pull_policy: never`,
103: so a plain `docker compose up -d` will not build or pull it; it just fails
104: or runs a stale image. You must build it on the host after any code change,
105: then recreate the containers. This is the #1 cause of "it won't pick up my
106: change" confusion on this project.
107: 
108: Full required-env-var table, the `install.ps1`/`install.sh` build-and-deploy
109: steps, and how to verify a worker is streaming to the right place:
110: **[docs/deployment.md](docs/deployment.md)**.
111: 
112: ## Configuration
113: 
114: All runtime behavior is config-driven — no code changes needed to retune an
115: agent. `config/worker.yaml` is the canonical annotated template of every
116: worker parameter that exists; per-role configs (`config/workers/*.yaml`)
117: only ever override a subset of it, and environment variables override those
118: at runtime.
119: 
120: Full config-section reference, worker on/off control internals, and the
121: config-driven tmux layout system (which maps directly onto Kubernetes
122: ConfigMaps): **[docs/configuration.md](docs/configuration.md)**.
123: 
124: ## Project Structure
125: 
126: Top level:
127: 
128: - `app/` — agent loop, LLM/TTS/coding-backend clients, avatar rendering, Rerun Theater
129: - `services/` — `message-logger`, `message-api`, `control-panel`, `twitch-presence`
130: - `sandbox/` — seeded-bug workspace the coder agents actually code on
131: - `repos/` — vendored third-party avatar repos
132: - `config/` — worker configs, tmux panel/layout presets
133: - `docs/` — per-module reference docs, including this README's detail subfiles
134: - `tests/` — pytest suite
135: - `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `startup.sh`, `requirements.txt`, `.env.example` — root-level build/run files
136: 
137: Full annotated tree, one line per file: **[docs/project_structure.md](docs/project_structure.md)**.
138: 
139: > **Note:** the generic "Mafober Deployment Environment" section below is shared
140: > boilerplate synced across every project on this machine, describing the default
141: > homelab deploy target for *new* projects. It does not apply to virtualTubers —
142: > this project's actual deployment target is **d2000** (`192.168.2.158`), documented
143: > in [docs/deployment.md](docs/deployment.md) above.

152: ## Claude Code Hook: .venv Enforcement
153: 
154: This project's `.claude/settings.json` includes a `PreToolUse` hook (matcher
155: `Bash|PowerShell`) that blocks Claude Code from invoking the global/system
156: Python directly — bare `python`, `python3`, `pip`, `pip3` — whenever a
157: `.venv` directory exists at the project root. It's a no-op in projects
158: without a `.venv`. Commands that go through `.venv\Scripts\...` /
159: `.venv/bin/...` directly, or that activate the venv within the same command,
160: are unaffected.
161: 
162: This exists because the "always use `.venv`, never global Python" rule was
163: already documented (see above and in CLAUDE.md) but was still being followed
164: inconsistently when left to memory/instructions alone — a hook enforces it
165: at the tool-call level instead of relying on the model to remember. Any
166: project with a `.venv` can adopt the same hook; see this project's
167: `.claude/settings.json` for the exact hook definition to copy.
168: 
169: 
170: ## Mafober Deployment Environment
171: 
172: New projects created or cloned into the managed projects root (`projects_root` in `config.yaml`) deploy to **mafober**, a Proxmox VE homelab host that also runs the shared Docker/Portainer stack for this machine.
173: 
174: ### Connection
175: 
176: | Item | Value |
177: |------|-------|
178: | Hostname | `mafober` |
179: | IP Address | `192.168.1.117` |
180: | Proxmox Web UI | `https://192.168.1.117:8006` |
181: | Portainer (Docker mgmt) | `https://192.168.1.120:9443` |
182: | SSH / SFTP | port `22` on `192.168.1.117` |
183: 
184: ### Deploying a new project
185: 
186: 1. Create a ZFS dataset under `tank_0` for the project's persistent storage (`zfs create tank_0/utilities/<project>`) rather than relying on ephemeral CT storage or named Docker volumes.
187: 2. `chown` the new dataset to the UID/GID the container image expects (e.g. `1000:1000` for linuxserver images, `472:472` for Grafana-style images).
188: 3. Add an explicit bind mount for the dataset into CT 101 (the Portainer LXC): `pct set 101 -mp<N> /tank_0/utilities/<project>,mp=/tank_0/utilities/<project>`, then `pct restart 101`. Each ZFS dataset needs its own `mp` entry — mounting a parent dataset does not expose its children.
189: 4. Define the stack/container in Portainer (`https://192.168.1.120:9443`) pointing at the bind-mounted path.
190: 5. If the project should be scraped by Prometheus or shipped logs to Grafana, register it alongside the existing dashboards/exporters on the host.
191: 
192: ### Currently deployed on mafober
193: 
194: - **Portainer** — Docker/stack management (CT 101)
195: - **Plex** — media server
196: - **qBittorrent** — torrent client
197: - **Grafana** — dashboards
198: - **Prometheus** — metrics
199: - **node_exporter** / **zfs_exporter** — host-level metrics, run directly on the Proxmox host (not containerized)
200: 
201: ### More info
202: 
203: Full hardware specs, ZFS layout, container configs, and troubleshooting lessons learned live in `mafober/mafober_summary.md` (a sibling project directory under the managed projects root). Check there first if these details aren't enough.
204: <!-- SHARED:END -->

1: """
2: Schema, parser, validator and prompt builder for the segment planner.
3: 
4: This module handles the pure half of Layer 2 of the 3-layer offline content
5: generator. It provides functions to parse model replies, validate chapters
6: and slots, build prompts for the segment planner, and merge the results into
7: brief.yaml format.
8: """
9: import logging
10: import re
11: import yaml
12: from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Union
13: 
14: log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
15: 
16: SYSTEM_PROMPT_CHAPTERS = (
17:     "You are a story architect planning a long-running serialized arc for a live "
18:     "virtual-streamer campaign. Your task is to break a 6-hour segment into "
19:     "chapters that will guide the generation of authored slots. Each chapter "
20:     "should be a self-contained unit with a clear beginning, middle, and end. "
21:     "The chapters must cover the entire segment's time span without gaps or overlaps."
22: )
23: 
24: SYSTEM_PROMPT_SLOTS = (
25:     "You are a story architect planning a long-running serialized arc for a live "
26:     "virtual-streamer campaign. Your task is to generate authored slots for one "
27:     "chapter of a 6-hour segment. Each slot should be a self-contained beat that "
28:     "can be generated as dialogue or narration, with clear participants and "
29:     "context. The slots must fit within the chapter's summary and contribute to "
30:     "the overall narrative arc."
31: )
32: 
33: class SegmentPlanError(ValueError):
34:     """Raised ONLY for a model reply that cannot be parsed into a list."""
35:     pass
36: 
37: 
38: def _parse_reply(reply: str, key: str) -> list:
39:     """
40:     Parse a model reply into a list of dictionaries.
41:     
42:     Implements the exact algorithm described in the specification:
43:       1. If the text contains a ``` fence, take the text BETWEEN the first
44:          fence and the next fence. Strip an optional language word
45:          ("yaml", "yml", "json") off the front of the first line.
46:       2. Otherwise use the whole text.
47:       3. `yaml.safe_load` it.
48:       4. If the result is a dict, return `result[key]` when that key is
49:          present and is a list.
50:       5. If the result is itself a list, return it.
51:       6. Otherwise raise SegmentPlanError.
52: 
53:     Args:
54:         reply: The raw model reply text
55:         key: The key to extract from the parsed dict (e.g., "chapters", "slots")
56: 
57:     Returns:
58:         A list of dictionaries
59: 
60:     Raises:
61:         SegmentPlanError: If parsing fails or result is not a list
62:     """
63:     log.debug("Parsing reply for key '%s'", key)
64:     text = reply.strip()
65: 
66:     # 1. If the text contains a ``` fence, take the text BETWEEN the first
67:     #    fence and the next fence. Strip an optional language word.
68:     m = re.search(r"```(?:yaml|yml|json)?\s*\n(.*?)```", text, re.DOTALL)
69:     if m:
70:         text = m.group(1)
71:         # Strip optional language from first line
72:         lines = text.splitlines()
73:         if lines and lines[0].strip() in ("yaml", "yml", "json"):
74:             text = "\n".join(lines[1:])
75: 
76:     # 2. Otherwise use the whole text (already done above)
77: 
78:     # 3. yaml.safe_load it
79:     try:
80:         parsed = yaml.safe_load(text)
81:     except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
82:         log.debug("YAML parsing failed: %s", exc)
83:         raise SegmentPlanError(f"Failed to parse YAML: {exc}")
84: 
85:     # 4. If the result is a dict, return result[key] when that key is present and is a list
86:     if isinstance(parsed, dict):
87:         if key not in parsed:
88:             raise SegmentPlanError(f"Reply does not contain '{key}' key")
89:         result = parsed[key]
90:         if not isinstance(result, list):
91:             raise SegmentPlanError(f"'{key}' must be a list, got {type(result).__name__}")
92:         # Check that all elements are dicts
93:         for i, item in enumerate(result):
94:             if not isinstance(item, dict):
95:                 raise SegmentPlanError(f"Element {i} of '{key}' is not a dictionary")
96:         return result
97: 
98:     # 5. If the result is itself a list, return it
99:     if isinstance(parsed, list):
100:         # Check that all elements are dicts
101:         for i, item in enumerate(parsed):
102:             if not isinstance(item, dict):
103:                 raise SegmentPlanError(f"Element {i} of reply is not a dictionary")
104:         return parsed
105: 
106:     # 6. Otherwise raise SegmentPlanError
107:     raise SegmentPlanError(f"Reply could not be parsed into a list: {type(parsed).__name__}")
108: 
109: 
110: def parse_chapters(reply) -> list:
111:     """Parse chapters from a model reply."""
112:     return _parse_reply(reply, "chapters")
113: 
114: 
115: def parse_slots(reply) -> list:
116:     """Parse slots from a model reply."""
117:     return _parse_reply(reply, "slots")
118: 
119: 
120: def validate_chapters(chapters, config) -> List[str]:
121:     """
122:     Validate a list of chapters.
123:     
124:     Returns a list of problem strings, [] when clean. Never raises.
125:     
126:     Args:
127:         chapters: List of chapter dictionaries
128:         config: Configuration dictionary
129:         
130:     Returns:
131:         List of problem strings
132:     """
133:     log.debug("Validating %d chapters", len(chapters))
134:     problems = []
135:     
136:     # Required keys check
137:     required = ["chapter_id", "order", "title", "summary",
138:                 "continuity_in", "continuity_out"]
139:     
140:     for ch in chapters:
141:         for key in required:
142:             if key not in ch:
143:                 problems.append(f"chapter is missing required key '{key}'")
144:     
145:     if problems:
146:         return problems
147:     
148:     # Length check
149:     expected_chapters = config["segment"]["chapters_per_segment"]
150:     if len(chapters) != expected_chapters:
151:         problems.append(
152:             f"expected {expected_chapters} chapters, got {len(chapters)}"
153:         )
154:     
155:     # Order values check
156:     orders = [ch["order"] for ch in chapters]
157:     expected_orders = set(range(len(chapters)))
158:     actual_orders = set(orders)
159:     if actual_orders != expected_orders:
160:         problems.append(
161:             f"chapter order values must be exactly {sorted(expected_orders)}, "
162:             f"got {sorted(actual_orders)}"
163:         )
164:     
165:     # Chapter_id uniqueness check
166:     chapter_ids = [ch["chapter_id"] for ch in chapters]
167:     if len(chapter_ids) != len(set(chapter_ids)):
168:         problems.append("chapter_id values must be unique")
169:     
170:     # Summary emptiness check
171:     for ch in chapters:
172:         if not isinstance(ch["summary"], str) or not ch["summary"].strip():
173:             problems.append(
174:                 f"chapter '{ch['chapter_id']}' summary must be a non-empty string"
175:             )
176:     
177:     return problems
178: 
179: 
180: def validate_slots(slots, pack, vocab, config) -> List[str]:
181:     """
182:     Validate a list of slots.
183:     
184:     Returns a list of problem strings, [] when clean. Never raises.
185:     
186:     Args:
187:         slots: List of slot dictionaries
188:         pack: CampaignPack object
189:         vocab: Vocabulary object
190:         config: Configuration dictionary
191:         
192:     Returns:
193:         List of problem strings
194:     """
195:     log.debug("Validating %d slots", len(slots))
196:     problems = []
197:     
198:     # Required keys check first, early return
199:     for slot in slots:
200:         kind = slot.get("kind")
201:         if kind == "ambient":
202:             required = ["slot_id", "kind", "prompt", "lore", "participants", 
203:                         "sensitivity", "depends_on"]
204:         elif kind == "spine":
205:             required = ["slot_id", "kind", "scene_ref", "participants"]
206:         else:
207:             problems.append(
208:                 f"slot '{slot.get('slot_id', '<no slot_id>')}' has unknown kind '{kind}'"
209:             )
210:             continue
211:             
212:         for key in required:
213:             if key not in slot:
214:                 problems.append(
215:                     f"slot '{slot.get('slot_id', '<no slot_id>')}' "
216:                     f"is missing required key '{key}'"
217:                 )
218:     
219:     if problems:
220:         return problems
221:     
222:     # Check each slot
223:     slot_ids = []
224:     for slot in slots:
225:         slot_id = slot["slot_id"]
226:         slot_ids.append(slot_id)
227:         
228:         # Delegate to vocab validation
229:         vocab_problems = vocab.validate_slot(slot)
230:         problems.extend(vocab_problems)
231:         
232:         # Skip further checks if this slot has vocab problems
233:         if any(p.startswith(f"slot '{slot_id}'") for p in vocab_problems):
234:             continue
235:             
236:         # Check participants
237:         participants = slot.get("participants")
238:         if not isinstance(participants, list):
239:             problems.append(
240:                 f"slot '{slot_id}' participants must be a list"
241:             )
242:         else:
243:             if len(participants) == 0:
244:                 problems.append(
245:                     f"slot '{slot_id}' participants must be a non-empty list"
246:                 )
247:             else:
248:                 for participant in participants:
249:                     if participant not in pack.cast:
250:                         problems.append(
251:                             f"slot '{slot_id}' has unknown participant '{participant}'"
252:                         )
253:         
254:         # Check kind-specific requirements
255:         if slot["kind"] == "ambient":
256:             # Check scene_ref is not present
257:             if "scene_ref" in slot:
258:                 problems.append(
259:                     f"slot '{slot_id}' cannot have a scene_ref key (only spine slots can)"
260:                 )
261:             
262:             # Check prompt
263:             prompt = slot.get("prompt")
264:             if not isinstance(prompt, str) or not prompt.strip():
265:                 problems.append(
266:                     f"slot '{slot_id}' prompt must be a non-empty string"
267:                 )
268:                 
269:             # Check depends_on is list
270:             depends_on = slot.get("depends_on")
271:             if not isinstance(depends_on, list):
272:                 problems.append(
273:                     f"slot '{slot_id}' depends_on must be a list"
274:                 )
275:             else:
276:                 # Check sensitivity and depends_on consistency
277:                 sensitivity = slot.get("sensitivity")
278:                 if sensitivity == "flags" and len(depends_on) == 0:
279:                     problems.append(
280:                         f"slot '{slot_id}' has sensitivity 'flags' but empty depends_on"
281:                     )
282:                 
283:                 # Check budget
284:                 takes = config["dialogue"]["takes_per_slot"]
285:                 neutral = config["dialogue"]["neutral_takes"]
286:                 conditioned = takes - neutral
287:                 if len(depends_on) * 2 > conditioned:
288:                     problems.append(
289:                         f"slot '{slot_id}' declares {len(depends_on)} dependencies "
290:                         f"but only {conditioned} conditioned takes available"
291:                     )
292:         
293:         elif slot["kind"] == "spine":
294:             # Check scene_ref exists in pack
295:             scene_ref = slot.get("scene_ref")
296:             if scene_ref is not None and scene_ref not in pack.scenes:
297:                 problems.append(
298:                     f"slot '{slot_id}' references unknown scene '{scene_ref}'"
299:                 )
300:     
301:     # Duplicate slot_id check
302:     if len(slot_ids) != len(set(slot_ids)):
303:         seen = set()
304:         for sid in slot_ids:
305:             if sid in seen:
306:                 problems.append(f"duplicate slot_id '{sid}' found")
307:             else:
308:                 seen.add(sid)
309:     
310:     return problems
311: 
312: 
313: def sensitivity_mix(slots) -> Dict[str, int]:
314:     """
315:     Count ambient slots by sensitivity type.
316:     
317:     Returns `{"none": n, "tone": n, "flags": n}` counting AMBIENT slots only.
318:     A slot with no `sensitivity` key is not counted at all.
319:     
320:     Args:
321:         slots: List of slot dictionaries
322:         
323:     Returns:
324:         Dictionary mapping sensitivity types to counts
325:     """
326:     mix = {"none": 0, "tone": 0, "flags": 0}
327:     
328:     for slot in slots:
329:         if slot.get("kind") == "ambient":
330:             sensitivity = slot.get("sensitivity")
331:             if sensitivity in mix:
332:                 mix[sensitivity] += 1
333:     
334:     return mix
335: 
336: 
337: def check_sensitivity_budget(slots, config) -> Optional[str]:
338:     """
339:     Check if the fraction of flags slots exceeds the budget.
340:     
341:     Returns a message string when the fraction of `flags` slots exceeds
342:     `config["segment"]["sensitivity_budget"]`, otherwise None.
343:     
344:     Args:
345:         slots: List of slot dictionaries
346:         config: Configuration dictionary
347:         
348:     Returns:
349:         Message string or None
350:     """
351:     mix = sensitivity_mix(slots)
352:     total = sum(mix.values())
353:     
354:     if total == 0:
355:         return None
356:     
357:     fraction = mix["flags"] / total
358:     budget = config["segment"]["sensitivity_budget"]
359:     
360:     if fraction <= budget:
361:         return None
362:     
363:     log.warning(
364:         "Flags slots (%d) exceed budget (%.2f) in fraction (%.2f)",
365:         mix["flags"], budget, fraction
366:     )
367:     return (f"{mix['flags']} of {total} slots are state-sensitive "
368:             f"({fraction:.2f}), over the budget of {budget}")
369: 
370: 
371: def build_chapter_prompt(pack, arc_segment, config, problems) -> str:
372:     """
373:     Build the 2a user prompt for chapter planning.
374:     
375:     Args:
376:         pack: CampaignPack object
377:         arc_segment: Segment dictionary
378:         config: Configuration dictionary
379:         problems: List of problem strings from previous attempt, or None
380:         
381:     Returns:
382:         The complete prompt string
383:     """
384:     chapters_per_segment = config["segment"]["chapters_per_segment"]
385:     order_range = f"0 to {chapters_per_segment - 1}"
386:     
387:     lines = [
388:         f"{arc_segment['synopsis']}",
389:         "",
390:         f"Continuity in: {arc_segment['continuity_in']}",
391:         f"Continuity out: {arc_segment['continuity_out']}",
392:         "",
393:         f"Plan {chapters_per_segment} chapters.",
394:         f"Chapter order must be exactly the integers {order_range}.",
395:         "",
396:         "Reply with ONLY YAML under a 'chapters:' key."
397:     ]
398:     
399:     if problems:
400:         lines.append("")
401:         lines.append("Previous attempt had these problems:")
402:         for problem in problems:
403:             lines.append(f"- {problem}")
404:     
405:     return "\n".join(lines)
406: 
407: 
408: def build_slot_prompt(pack, arc_segment, chapter, config, problems) -> str:
409:     """
410:     Build the 2b user prompt for slot planning.
411:     
412:     Args:
413:         pack: CampaignPack object
414:         arc_segment: Segment dictionary
415:         chapter: Chapter dictionary
416:         config: Configuration dictionary
417:         problems: List of problem strings from previous attempt, or None
418:         
419:     Returns:
420:         The complete prompt string
421:     """
422:     target_slots = round(config["segment"]["target_slots"] / 
423:                          config["segment"]["chapters_per_segment"])
424:     
425:     lines = [
426:         f"Chapter summary: {chapter['summary']}",
427:         "",
428:         f"Plan {target_slots} slots for this chapter.",
429:         "",
430:         "Legal state keys (for depends_on):",
431:     ]
432:     
433:     for flag in config["state"]["flags"]:
434:         lines.append(f"- {flag}")
435:     
436:     lines.append("")
437:     lines.append("Cast:")
438:     for player_id in pack.player_ids:
439:         member = pack.cast[player_id]
440:         lines.append(f"- {player_id}: {member.name}")
441:     
442:     lines.append("")
443:     lines.append("Spine ground truth (for the ear):")
444:     for scene_id in arc_segment["spine_scenes"]:
445:         if scene_id in pack.scenes:
446:             scene = pack.scenes[scene_id]
447:             if scene.enter_narration:
448:                 lines.append(f"- {scene_id}: {scene.enter_narration}")
449:     
450:     lines.append("")
451:     lines.append("Each slot's prompt must be written for the ear.")
452:     lines.append("A slot may declare at most one dependency in depends_on.")
453:     lines.append("")
454:     lines.append("Reply with ONLY YAML under a 'slots:' key.")
455:     
456:     if problems:
457:         lines.append("")
458:         lines.append("Previous attempt had these problems:")
459:         for problem in problems:
460:             lines.append(f"- {problem}")
461:     
462:     return "\n".join(lines)
463: 
464: 
465: def merge_brief(arc_segment, chapters, slots_by_chapter, config) -> Dict:
466:     """
467:     Merge chapters and slots into a brief dictionary.
468:     
469:     Args:
470:         arc_segment: Segment dictionary
471:         chapters: List of chapter dictionaries
472:         slots_by_chapter: Dictionary mapping chapter_id to list of slot dicts
473:         config: Configuration dictionary
474:         
475:     Returns:
476:         The merged brief dictionary
477:     """
478:     out_slots = []
479:     for ch in sorted(chapters, key=lambda c: c["order"]):
480:         for slot in slots_by_chapter.get(ch["chapter_id"], []):
481:             slot = dict(slot)
482:             slot["chapter_id"] = ch["chapter_id"]
483:             out_slots.append(slot)
484: 
485:     return {
486:         "segment_id": arc_segment["id"],
487:         "order": arc_segment["order"],
488:         "loop": arc_segment["loop"],
489:         "hours": arc_segment["hours"],
490:         "synopsis": arc_segment["synopsis"],
491:         "continuity_in": arc_segment["continuity_in"],
492:         "continuity_out": arc_segment["continuity_out"],
493:         "carry_in": arc_segment.get("carry_in", {}),
494:         "carry_out": arc_segment.get("carry_out", {}),
495:         "chapters": sorted(chapters, key=lambda c: c["order"]),
496:         "slots": out_slots,
497:     }

23: def plan_segment(pack, arc_segment, config, llm, vocab, out_path) -> Optional[Dict]:
24:     """

26:     
27:     Args:
28:         pack: The campaign pack

30:         config: Configuration dictionary

45:                 f"could not load existing brief {out_path}: {exc}") from exc

53:     # Step 2 - The 2a pass (skip entirely when chapters is already non-empty)

79: 
80:     # Step 3 - The 2b fan-out

94:         for attempt in range(config["segment"]["max_attempts"]):

104:                 problems = [str(exc)]

115: 
116:     # Plan missing chapters concurrently

119:         # Submit all tasks
