1: """
2: Scan-and-assign pass for the 3-layer offline content generator.
3: 
4: The existing batch generator allocates take numbers lazily: `batch_generate._next_take_number`
5: globs the directory for `*.yaml` and returns `max(...) + 1`. That is correct for one sequential
6: writer and broken for a pool of eight: two workers both see `002.yaml` as the highest,
7: both claim take 3, and one silently overwrites the other's work.
8: 
9: The fix is NOT a lock around the allocator. It is to stop allocating lazily.
10: Scan the output tree once, compute every `(segment, slot, take)` that is not
11: yet on disk, assign each one its deterministic path up front, and emit an
12: ordered list. Four things fall out of that single change:
13: 
14:   - race-freedom: every path is decided before any thread starts;
15:   - idempotent resume: re-running the scan IS the resume, because a take
16:     already on disk is simply not planned again;
17:   - a hole in the middle gets refilled — lazy allocation would see `003` as
18:     the max and plan take 4, leaving the missing `002` unfilled forever;
19:   - an exact work count and ETA before an operator commits to a two-day run.
20: 
21: This module is nearly pure: it reads the output tree (existence checks) and,
22: in `build_worklist`, reads each segment's `brief.yaml`. No LLM, no network,
23: no writes.
24: """
25: import logging
26: import pathlib
27: import yaml
28: from dataclasses import dataclass
29: from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Union
30: 
31: log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
32: 
33: 
34: class WorklistError(ValueError):
35:     """Raised for a malformed brief, a missing brief file, or a nonsensical estimate argument."""
36:     pass
37: 
38: 
39: @dataclass(frozen=True)
40: class WorkUnit:
41:     """A unit of work for the 3-layer generation pipeline."""
42:     segment_id: str
43:     slot_id: str
44:     take: int                    # 1-based
45:     conditions: dict             # {} means the neutral take
46:     path: pathlib.Path           # where this take will be written
47:     slot: dict                   # the whole slot from the brief
48: 
49: 
50: def take_path(segment_root: pathlib.Path, slot_id: str, take: int) -> pathlib.Path:
51:     """Returns `segment_root / "slots" / slot_id / f"{take:03d}.yaml"`."""
52:     return segment_root / "slots" / slot_id / f"{take:03d}.yaml"
53: 
54: 
55: def conditions_for(slot: dict, take: int, config: dict) -> dict:
56:     """
57:     The condition mapping for a 1-based `take` on one ambient slot.
58:     
59:     Rules, in order:
60:       - `take <= neutral_takes`  -> `{}`. Always. No exceptions.
61:       - slot["sensitivity"] == "none"  -> `{}` for every take. The line
62:         reads the same in any state; conditioning it would only shrink
63:         the pool the runtime can draw from.
64:       - otherwise build an ordered CANDIDATE list and index into it with
65:         `(take - neutral_takes - 1) % len(candidates)`:
66:           * sensitivity "tone": one candidate per declared mood, in
67:             config order — `{"mood": m}` for each m in
68:             config["state"]["moods"].
69:           * sensitivity "flags": for each flag in slot["depends_on"], IN
70:             ORDER, two candidates: `{flag: True}` then `{flag: False}`.
71:             Both polarities of the first flag come before the second
72:             flag's — with the usual budget of two conditioned takes and
73:             one dependency, the useful split is true/false.
74:       - if the candidate list is EMPTY (e.g. sensitivity "flags" with an
75:         empty `depends_on`, or "tone" with no declared moods), return
76:         `{}`. Nothing to condition on; a neutral take is still a useful
77:         take.
78: 
79:     The modulo means more takes than distinct conditions is NOT an error
80:     — a second take under the same condition is ordinary variety.
81:     """
82:     # Default neutral_takes to 1 if not specified
83:     neutral_takes = config.get("dialogue", {}).get("neutral_takes", 1)
84:     
85:     # Take 1 is ALWAYS neutral
86:     if take <= neutral_takes:
87:         return {}
88:     
89:     # No conditioning if sensitivity is "none"
90:     if slot.get("sensitivity") == "none":
91:         return {}
92:     
93:     candidates = []
94:     
95:     # Build candidate list based on sensitivity
96:     if slot.get("sensitivity") == "tone":
97:         moods = config.get("state", {}).get("moods", [])
98:         candidates = [{"mood": mood} for mood in moods]
99:     elif slot.get("sensitivity") == "flags":
100:         depends_on = slot.get("depends_on", [])
101:         for flag in depends_on:
102:             candidates.extend([{flag: True}, {flag: False}])
103:     
104:     # If no candidates, return neutral
105:     if not candidates:
106:         return {}
107:     
108:     # Index into candidates with modulo
109:     index = (take - neutral_takes - 1) % len(candidates)
110:     return candidates[index]
111: 
112: 
113: def units_for_segment(segment_id: str, brief: dict, segment_root: pathlib.Path,
114:                       config: dict) -> List[WorkUnit]:
115:     """
116:     Plan one segment. `brief` is the loaded brief mapping; `segment_root`
117:     is the directory that segment's output lives under.
118:     
119:       - `brief["slots"]` is required. A brief with no "slots" key raises
120:         WorklistError — an empty worklist and a malformed brief look
121:         identical from the outside, and a two-day run must not exit in
122:         three seconds reporting success.
123:       - Slots with `kind == "spine"` produce NO units: spine dialogue is
124:         authored in the campaign pack, and Layer 3 only fills ambient.
125:       - Every other slot must have a "slot_id"; a slot without one raises
126:         WorklistError naming the segment.
127:       - For each ambient slot, for take in 1..config["dialogue"]["takes_per_slot"]:
128:         SKIP the take if `take_path(...)` already exists on disk;
129:         otherwise emit a WorkUnit.
130:     Order: slots in brief order, takes ascending within a slot. Draining
131:     in that order means stopping the run at any point leaves a contiguous
132:     usable prefix of airtime rather than a scattered fraction.
133: 
134:     Condition assignment keys off the TAKE NUMBER, never off position in
135:     the returned list. If take 1 already exists, the first planned unit is
136:     take 2 and it must still carry take 2's condition.
137:     """
138:     log.debug("units_for_segment called with segment_id=%s", segment_id)
139:     
140:     if "slots" not in brief:
141:         msg = f"brief for segment {segment_id} has no 'slots' key"
142:         log.error(msg)
143:         raise WorklistError(msg)
144:     
145:     takes_per_slot = config.get("dialogue", {}).get("takes_per_slot", 3)
146:     
147:     units = []
148:     planned = 0
149:     skipped = 0
150:     
151:     for slot in brief["slots"]:
152:         # Skip spine slots
153:         if slot.get("kind") == "spine":
154:             continue
155:             
156:         # Validate slot has an ID
157:         if "slot_id" not in slot:
158:             msg = f"segment {segment_id} has a slot without 'slot_id'"
159:             log.error(msg)
160:             raise WorklistError(msg)
161:             
162:         slot_id = slot["slot_id"]
163:         slot_root = segment_root / "slots" / slot_id
164:         
165:         # For each take, check if it exists and plan accordingly
166:         for take in range(1, takes_per_slot + 1):
167:             path = take_path(segment_root, slot_id, take)
168:             
169:             # If the file already exists, skip it
170:             if path.exists():
171:                 skipped += 1
172:                 continue
173:                 
174:             conditions = conditions_for(slot, take, config)
175:             unit = WorkUnit(
176:                 segment_id=segment_id,
177:                 slot_id=slot_id,
178:                 take=take,
179:                 conditions=conditions,
180:                 path=path,
181:                 slot=slot
182:             )
183:             units.append(unit)
184:             planned += 1
185:     
186:     log.debug("segment %s: planned %d, skipped %d", segment_id, planned, skipped)
187:     return units
188: 
189: 
190: def build_worklist(output_root: pathlib.Path, segment_ids: List[str],
191:                    config: dict) -> List[WorkUnit]:
192:     """
193:     The whole-run pass. For each segment id IN THE GIVEN ORDER, read
194:     `output_root / "segments" / <segment_id> / "brief.yaml"` with
195:     `yaml.safe_load`, then delegate to `units_for_segment` with
196:     `segment_root = output_root / "segments" / <segment_id>`.
197:     Concatenate.
198: 
199:     A missing or unreadable brief raises WorklistError naming the
200:     segment, logged at ERROR first.
201:     """
202:     log.debug("build_worklist called with output_root=%s, segment_ids=%s", output_root, segment_ids)
203:     
204:     units = []
205:     
206:     for segment_id in segment_ids:
207:         segment_root = output_root / "segments" / segment_id
208:         brief_path = segment_root / "brief.yaml"
209:         
210:         try:
211:             with open(brief_path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
212:                 brief = yaml.safe_load(f)
213:         except OSError as exc:
214:             msg = f"could not read brief for segment {segment_id}: {exc}"
215:             log.error(msg)
216:             raise WorklistError(msg) from exc
217:         except yaml.YAMLError as exc:
218:             msg = f"malformed YAML in brief for segment {segment_id}: {exc}"
219:             log.error(msg)
220:             raise WorklistError(msg) from exc
221:             
222:         segment_units = units_for_segment(segment_id, brief, segment_root, config)
223:         units.extend(segment_units)
224:     
225:     log.info("build_worklist completed: %d total units", len(units))
226:     return units
227: 
228: 
229: def estimate_seconds(units: List[WorkUnit], seconds_per_take: float,
230:                      concurrency: int) -> float:
231:     """
232:     `len(units) * seconds_per_take / concurrency`. An empty list is 0.
233:     A `concurrency` less than 1 raises WorklistError — a divide-by-zero
234:     traceback is a poor way to learn the config is wrong.
235:     """
236:     log.debug("estimate_seconds called with units=%d, seconds_per_take=%f, concurrency=%d",
237:               len(units), seconds_per_take, concurrency)
238:     
239:     if concurrency < 1:
240:         msg = f"concurrency must be at least 1, got {concurrency}"
241:         log.error(msg)
242:         raise WorklistError(msg)
243:         
244:     return len(units) * seconds_per_take / concurrency

1: """
2: Worker pool that drains the worklist for the 3-layer offline content generator.
3: 
4: This module is where three separate hazards meet. Each one is a real failure
5: that has a specific, non-obvious fix.
6: 
7: 1. CONTEXT BLEED. `LLMImproviser` (app/campaign/improviser.py) is NOT
8:    thread-safe: it holds mutable `scene`, `carry`, `loop` and `recent`
9:    attributes that `update_context` rewrites on every take. Sharing one
10:    across eight workers splices one slot's transcript into another slot's
11:    prompt. The output is not a crash — it is takes that read as
12:    non-sequiturs, with nothing in the log to say why. The fix is ONE
13:    improviser per WORKER THREAD (not per unit — constructing one per unit
14:    would re-read the pack tens of thousands of times). The pack and the LLM
15:    client are read-only and stay shared.
16: 
17: 2. INTERLEAVED WRITES. Every worker appending to the same manifest JSONL
18:    corrupts it. The fix is not a lock: workers RETURN results, and ONE
19:    dedicated writer thread performs every write. That also gives clean crash
20:    semantics — the file is only ever touched by one thread.
21: 
22: 3. SILENT FAILURE. `LLMImproviser.generate_scene` catches EVERY exception and
23:    returns `[]`. A dead ollama, an OOM, or a wrong model name therefore
24:    produces an endless stream of empty takes AT FULL SPEED, and the run looks
25:    healthy the whole time. The fix is a rolling failure-rate circuit breaker
26:    that aborts the run. It is the difference between losing ten minutes and
27:    losing two days.
28: """
29: import collections
30: import logging
31: import threading
32: import queue
33: from dataclasses import dataclass
34: from typing import List, Callable, Any, Optional
35: 
36: log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
37: 
38: 
39: class PoolError(RuntimeError):
40:     """Raised for a nonsensical configuration (concurrency < 1, an invalid breaker window)."""
41:     pass
42: 
43: 
44: class CircuitBreakerTripped(PoolError):
45:     """Raised out of `run_pool` when the breaker trips. It is deliberately
46:     an exception and not a return value: the caller must not be able to
47:     mistake an aborted run for a completed one.
48:     """
49:     pass
50: 
51: 
52: class CircuitBreaker:
53:     def __init__(self, window: int, failure_rate: float, min_samples: int):
54:         if window < 1:
55:             raise PoolError("window must be at least 1")
56:         
57:         self.window = window
58:         self.failure_rate = failure_rate
59:         self.min_samples = min_samples
60:         self._outcomes = collections.deque(maxlen=window)
61:         self._lock = threading.Lock()
62: 
63:     def record(self, ok: bool) -> None:
64:         """Append one outcome. MUST be thread-safe: eight workers call it
65:         concurrently. Guard the deque with a `threading.Lock`.
66:         """
67:         with self._lock:
68:             self._outcomes.append(ok)
69: 
70:     @property
71:     def tripped(self) -> bool:
72:         """True when BOTH:
73:           - at least `min_samples` outcomes have been recorded in the
74:             current window, AND
75:           - the failure fraction within the window is >= `failure_rate`.
76:         Below `min_samples` it is always False. Three failures out of
77:         three is 100% and is not evidence; aborting a two-day run on it
78:         would be worse than the bug.
79:         Because the deque is bounded, old failures age out on their own —
80:         a rough patch at hour two must not abort a run that has since
81:         recovered.
82:         """
83:         with self._lock:
84:             if len(self._outcomes) < self.min_samples:
85:                 return False
86:             
87:             failures = sum(1 for outcome in self._outcomes if not outcome)
88:             rate = failures / len(self._outcomes)
89:             return rate >= self.failure_rate
90: 
91: 
92: @dataclass
93: class PoolStats:
94:     planned: int
95:     written: int
96:     failed: int
97: 
98: 
99: def run_pool(units, *, worker_factory, generate, writer,
100:              concurrency=4, max_attempts=2, breaker=None) -> PoolStats:
101:     """
102:     Run the worker pool that drains the worklist for the 3-layer offline content generator.
103: 
104:     `units`        an ordered sequence of worklist.WorkUnit.
105:     `worker_factory()`  called ONCE PER WORKER THREAD, returns that
106:                    thread's private context object (in production, an
107:                    LLMImproviser). Never call it per unit, and never
108:                    share one result between threads.
109:     `generate(worker, unit)`  returns a list of beats. An empty list, or
110:                    any exception, is a FAILED attempt.
111:     `writer(unit, beats)`  called ONLY on the single writer thread.
112:     `concurrency`  number of worker threads. < 1 raises PoolError.
113:     `max_attempts` attempts per unit before it is counted failed.
114:                    Default 2 — a single transient timeout should not
115:                    cost a slot its take.
116:     `breaker`      a CircuitBreaker, or None to disable the check.
117: 
118:     THE WORKER LOOP — implement exactly this. Each worker thread:
119: 
120:         worker = worker_factory()          # ONCE, before the loop
121:         while True:
122:             if stop_event.is_set(): break
123:             unit = work_queue.get()        # blocking, no timeout
124:             if unit is None: break         # its sentinel
125:             if stop_event.is_set(): break
126: 
127:             beats = None
128:             for attempt in range(1, max_attempts + 1):
129:                 try:
130:                     result = generate(worker, unit)
131:                 except Exception as exc:
132:                     log.error("unit %s attempt %d failed: %s",
133:                               unit.slot_id, attempt, exc)
134:                     continue                # next attempt
135:                 if result:                  # non-empty == success
136:                     beats = result
137:                     break
138:                 log.debug("unit %s attempt %d produced no beats",
139:                           unit.slot_id, attempt)
140: 
141:             ok = beats is not None
142:             if ok:
143:                 results_queue.put((unit, beats))
144:             else:
145:                 log.error("unit %s failed after %d attempts",
146:                           unit.slot_id, max_attempts)
147:                 with stats_lock:
148:                     stats.failed += 1
149: 
150:             if breaker is not None:
151:                 breaker.record(ok)          # EXACTLY ONCE PER UNIT
152:                 if breaker.tripped:
153:                     stop_event.set()
154:                     break                   # do NOT raise in the thread
155: 
156:     Note the four things that loop gets right, each of which a naive
157:     version gets wrong:
158: 
159:       - `breaker.record(ok)` is called ONCE PER UNIT, after all attempts
160:         — not once per attempt. A unit that succeeds on retry is a
161:         success, and counting its first attempt as a failure would trip
162:         the breaker on a perfectly healthy run.
163:       - `stats.failed` is incremented at the point of failure. Do not
164:         leave it to be reconstructed later; there is no later.
165:       - A `generate` exception `continue`s to the next attempt rather
166:         than abandoning the unit, and NEVER escapes the worker. A worker
167:         that dies on its first bad unit silently shrinks the pool for the
168:         rest of the run.
169:       - The breaker sets `stop_event` and BREAKS. It does not raise
170:         inside the worker thread — an exception there cannot reach the
171:         caller and would only skip the thread's own cleanup. `run_pool`
172:         raises on the main thread after the joins.
173: 
174:     `stats.written` is incremented by the WRITER thread, not here — only
175:     a write that actually succeeded counts as written.
176: 
177:     Read only the breaker's public surface (`record`, `tripped`). Do not
178:     reach into its internals for the log message; log the unit count and
179:     the configured rate instead.
180: 
181:     Dispatch units IN THE GIVEN ORDER: fill the queue in order before
182:     starting any thread. Stopping the run at any point must leave a
183:     contiguous usable prefix of airtime rather than a scattered fraction
184:     that cannot air.
185: 
186:     THE WRITER THREAD: one thread, started before the workers, looping
187:     `item = results_queue.get()`, breaking on the `None` sentinel, else
188:     calling `writer(unit, beats)`. On success `stats.written += 1`; on
189:     exception, log at ERROR and `stats.failed += 1` — a full disk must
190:     not stall the run.
191: 
192:     THE ENDING — implement exactly this, in exactly this order, AFTER
193:     the joins (see the shutdown protocol in the notes):
194: 
195:         log.info("pool completed: %d written, %d failed",
196:                  stats.written, stats.failed)
197: 
198:         if breaker is not None and breaker.tripped:
199:             log.critical("circuit breaker tripped after %d units "
200:                          "(threshold %.2f)", stats.planned,
201:                          breaker.failure_rate)
202:             raise CircuitBreakerTripped(...)
203: 
204:         return stats
205: 
206:     The INFO line comes FIRST, on every path — including the aborted
207:     one. Do not put it on the success path only. An abort is precisely
208:     the case where the operator most needs the count: they come back to
209:     a run that stopped early and the first question is "how much of the
210:     week is usable?" A partial arc of real takes is salvageable; zero is
211:     a restart. Logging the summary only when nothing went wrong answers
212:     that question exactly when it did not need asking, and stays silent
213:     when it did.
214: 
215:     CRITICAL, not ERROR, for the trip: ERROR is already the noise floor
216:     of a failing run, and only CRITICAL distinguishes "the run gave up"
217:     from "one slot misbehaved".
218: 
219:     `stats.written` and `stats.failed` are touched from several threads.
220:     Guard every mutation with a single `threading.Lock`.
221:     """
222:     if concurrency < 1:
223:         raise PoolError("concurrency must be at least 1")
224:     
225:     log.info("starting pool with %d units, %d workers", len(units), concurrency)
226:     
227:     # Setup queues and shared state
228:     work_queue = queue.Queue()
229:     results_queue = queue.Queue()
230:     stop_event = threading.Event()
231:     stats = PoolStats(planned=len(units), written=0, failed=0)
232:     stats_lock = threading.Lock()
233:     
234:     # Fill the work queue in order
235:     for unit in units:
236:         work_queue.put(unit)
237:     
238:     # Add sentinels for workers
239:     for _ in range(concurrency):
240:         work_queue.put(None)
241:     
242:     # Start writer thread first
243:     def writer_thread():
244:         while True:
245:             item = results_queue.get()
246:             if item is None:
247:                 break
248:             
249:             unit, beats = item
250:             try:
251:                 writer(unit, beats)
252:                 with stats_lock:
253:                     stats.written += 1
254:             except Exception as exc:
255:                 log.error("failed to write unit %s: %s", unit.slot_id, exc)
256:                 with stats_lock:
257:                     stats.failed += 1
258:     
259:     writer_thread_obj = threading.Thread(target=writer_thread)
260:     writer_thread_obj.start()
261:     
262:     # Start worker threads
263:     def worker_thread():
264:         worker = worker_factory()
265:         while True:
266:             if stop_event.is_set():
267:                 break
268:             
269:             try:
270:                 unit = work_queue.get(timeout=0.1)

273:                 
274:             if unit is None:

283:                     result = generate(worker, unit)
284:                 except Exception as exc:
285:                     log.error("unit %s attempt %d failed: %s",
286:                               unit.slot_id, attempt, exc)
287:                     continue
288:                 
289:                 if result:  # non-empty == success
290:                     beats = result
291:                     break
292:                 
293:                 log.debug("unit %s attempt %d produced no beats",
294:                           unit.slot_id, attempt)
295: 
296:             ok = beats is not None
297:             if ok:
298:                 results_queue.put((unit, beats))
299:             else:
300:                 log.error("unit %s failed after %d attempts",
301:                           unit.slot_id, max_attempts)
302:                 with stats_lock:
303:                     stats.failed += 1
304: 
305:             if breaker is not None:
306:                 breaker.record(ok)  # EXACTLY ONCE PER UNIT
307:                 if breaker.tripped:
308:                     stop_event.set()
309:                     break  # do NOT raise in the thread
310:     
311:     threads = []
312:     for _ in range(concurrency):
313:         thread = threading.Thread(target=worker_thread)
314:         thread.start()
315:         threads.append(thread)
316:     
317:     # Wait for all workers to finish
318:     for thread in threads:
319:         thread.join()
320:     
321:     # Close writer thread
322:     results_queue.put(None)
323:     writer_thread_obj.join()
324:     
325:     log.info("pool completed: %d written, %d failed",
326:              stats.written, stats.failed)
327: 
328:     if breaker is not None and breaker.tripped:
329:         log.critical("circuit breaker tripped after %d units "
330:                      "(threshold %.2f)", stats.planned,
331:                      breaker.failure_rate)
332:         raise CircuitBreakerTripped("Circuit breaker tripped")
333: 
334:     return stats

## Part A — Layer 2 recursive tree

| # | Component | What to build |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | `config/generation.yaml` `tree:` block | Replace `chapters_per_segment` with `max_leaf_slots / max_children / max_depth / min_node_words / leaf_density_floor` |
| A2 | `segment_schema.py` rewrite | New prompt constants (`SYSTEM_PROMPT_EXPAND`/`LEAF`); `parse_children`; `leaf_eligible`, `derive_target_slots`, `distribute_words` (exact-sum rounding); `validate_tree_config` (knob interlock, raises), `validate_children` (weight floor, non-empty continuity); `validate_slots(+node)` adding per-leaf density check; `build_expand_prompt`/`build_leaf_prompt` with **ancestor chain** + M2 prompt content (legal lore stems from `pack.lore`, spine beat text, `target_words` stop condition); `check_sensitivity_budget` unchanged |
| A3 | `merge_brief` tree-ification | DFS flatten of tree → same `brief.yaml` contract; slot provenance `node_id` (replaces `chapter_id`); new `nodes:` summary, `roll_points:` list (M3), `needs_rebrief:` flag (M5) |
| A4 | `plan_segment.py` continuous work queue | Root seed; `validate_tree_config` at startup; `tree.yaml` resume (derived path, signature unchanged); `while pending:` drain over one `ThreadPoolExecutor` with branch→children enqueued mid-drain; branch task (expand→parse→validate→distribute, retry→**forced-leaf degrade**); leaf task (leaf→parse→validate, retry→ERROR+empty); per-node checkpoint (tree.yaml + re-merged brief); final checks: segment-level sensitivity on flattened slots, slot-count + **word-count** density floor (M4), tree-shape summary log |
| A5 | Tests (before code, per decision 4) | `tests/segment_helpers.py` fixtures; `tests/test_segment_schema.py` (thresholds, exact-sum reconciliation, floor rejections, interlock, depth-3 ancestor rendering); `tests/test_plan_segment.py` (lopsided-depth asymmetry, max_depth valve, **expand-exhaustion → forced leaf with slots**, mid-drain resume, children enqueued into same pool) |
| A6 | qwen worker specs + regeneration | Rewrite `specs/3layers_segment_schema.yaml` + `3layers_plan_segment.yaml` (incl. M1 parallelism precondition verbatim), run with `--model qwen3.8:27b`, review, promote; `runner.py` order: schema then plan_segment |

## Part B — CLI (small, unblocks everything)

| # | Component | What to build |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | `main.py` orchestrator | argparse per the flag list; `--stage arc/segment/dialogue/all`; calls `plan_arc` / `plan_segment` / Layer 3; `validate_profile_for_stages` + surface error (closes issue #10) |

For Part C's neutral-take guard, I'm working through the logic: the guard checks that every ambient slot has exactly one take with empty conditions. Take 1 is always neutral, so as long as a complete run has take 1 present, the guard passes. The guard would fail if a slot's take 1 is missing or if some unexpected slot got a neutral take.

Part A's test ordering is already handled by the plan—tests exist before the qwen worker runs. For Part C, the dialogue stage needs to use `concurrent_llm.from_profile` to build the LLM client for the improviser, which is already available in the src package.

184: def _safe_name(name: str) -> str:
185:     """Apply the validator's name rule to a path parameter, so a lookup or a
186:     delete can never be handed something the upload path would have refused."""
187:     try:

194: async def upload_replay(
195:     request: Request,
196:     name: Optional[str] = Query(
197:         None, description="Override the episode key; defaults to the script's 'source'"),
198:     overwrite: bool = Query(
199:         False, description="Replace an episode of the same name instead of failing with 409"),
200: ):
201:     """Validate a pre-built episode script (scripts/build_replay_library.py)
202:     and store it in the library. The body is the raw episode JSON, so
203:     `curl --data-binary @episode.json` uploads one directly.
204: 
205:     Reads the body via `request.body()` rather than a `bytes = Body(...)`
206:     param on purpose: on fastapi>=0.14x, a `bytes`-typed Body param gets
207:     JSON-decoded before its own type validator runs whenever the client's
208:     Content-Type is application/json — which turned the documented
209:     `-H 'Content-Type: application/json' --data-binary @file` upload into a
210:     422, regardless of any `media_type=` hint passed to Body(). Reading
211:     straight from the ASGI request bypasses that per-field coercion
212:     entirely and always returns the raw bytes; json.loads() below is what
213:     actually parses it."""
214:     _require_store()
215:     body = await request.body()
216:     if len(body) > MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES:
217:         raise HTTPException(
218:             status_code=413,
219:             detail=f"episode is {len(body)} bytes, over the {MAX_UPLOAD_BYTES} byte limit")
220:     try:
221:         script = json.loads(body)
222:     except ValueError as exc:
223:         raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=f"body is not valid json: {exc}")
224: 
225:     try:
226:         # Raises EpisodeInvalid on bad shape, a bad name, a leak-audit hit or
227:         # a failed dry-run render. Its message is operator-facing and, by
228:         # construction, never quotes episode content.
229:         info = validate_episode(script, name=name)
230:     except EpisodeInvalid as exc:
231:         raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(exc))
232: 
233:     _ensure_schema()
234:     try:
235:         created = episode_store.save_episode(
236:             info["name"], script, overwrite=overwrite)
237:     except psycopg2.OperationalError as exc:
238:         raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail=f"postgres unavailable: {exc}")
239:     if not created:
240:         raise HTTPException(
241:             status_code=409,
242:             detail=f"episode {info['name']!r} already exists — re-send with ?overwrite=true")
243:     log.info("stored episode name=%s events=%s bytes=%s overwrite=%s",
244:              info["name"], info["event_count"], info["byte_size"], overwrite)
245:     return {**info, "created": True}
246: 
247:

254:     except psycopg2.OperationalError as exc:
255:         raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail=f"postgres unavailable: {exc}")

260:     """The full stored script, for debugging what a worker will perform."""
261:     _require_store()
262:     _ensure_schema()

265:     except psycopg2.OperationalError as exc:
266:         raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail=f"postgres unavailable: {exc}")
267:     if script is None:
268:         raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"no episode named {name!r}")
269:     return script

279:     except psycopg2.OperationalError as exc:
280:         raise HTTPException(status_code=503, detail=f"postgres unavailable: {exc}")
281:     return {"name": safe, "deleted": deleted}

## Assistant (Plan · qwen3.8:27b · 922.2s)

_Thinking:_

Now I have the full picture. Let me summarize what I've found:

1. **FastAPI + uvicorn** is the project's HTTP stack standard (message-api, control-panel use it).
2. `scripts/campaign_api.py` is just a CLI wrapper around `app/campaign/cli.py` (the *show* player), not the 3-layer generator. It's unrelated to what we're building.
3. The 3-layer generator code (`utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/src/`) is a set of pure-ish Python functions — no HTTP layer at all, no FastAPI, no server. It's invoked as a script (no `main.py` yet — that's Part B we were about to build).
4. The existing services (`message-api`, `control-panel`, `log-shipper`, `twitch-presence`) are all separate FastAPI apps, each with their own `requirements.txt`, run as their own Docker service in `docker-compose.yml`.
5. No existing async job/queue pattern in the repo (the search results were false positives — `run_pool` is the threaded pool in `pool.py`, not a web job queue).

So the question is: **what does "API" look like here?**

The 3-layer generator has three stages (arc, segment, dialogue) that are long-running (minutes to days for a full run). The CLI design was:
- `--stage arc` → plan the arc (minutes)
- `--stage segment` → plan all segments (hours)
- `--stage dialogue` → generate all takes (days)
- `--dry-run` → preview
- `--test-mode` → run one segment per profile (minutes)

As an API, the natural shape is:
- **Async job submission** — POST `/jobs` with a stage/pack/profile → returns a `job_id`
- **Job status** — GET `/jobs/{job_id}` → status, progress, logs
- **Stop job** — POST `/jobs/{job_id}/stop`
- **List available packs** — GET `/packs`
- **List profile names** — GET `/profiles` or part of job config
- **Dry-run preview** — POST `/dry-run` (synchronous, returns tree shape immediately)
- **Health** — GET `/healthz`

This is a **new FastAPI service**, following the same pattern as `message-api` and `control-panel`. It should live under `services/3layer-generator/` (or similar) with its own `requirements.txt` and compose service entry.

The key design decisions to surface:
1. **Job model**: in-memory only (resets on restart, acceptable for a single-operator tool) vs persisted in Postgres (survives restarts, queryable from GUI). Given the project already has Postgres for episodes and message logs, and the GUI will want to see historical runs, Postgres seems right. But that's more infrastructure.
2. **Where the service lives**: `services/3layer-generator/` as a new compose service.
3. **How it drives the generation**: the existing `src/` modules are synchronous/threaded (not async-native). The service wraps them in a background thread and tracks state — no need to make the core modules async.
4. **Progress reporting**: the pool's `PoolStats` (written/failed) gives natural progress; the segment planner can report per-segment completion.

Let me think about what to present to the user. The main question is: **job persistence — Postgres or in-memory?**

Actually, looking at this more carefully, I think the right answer is:
- **In-memory job tracking** for the MVP: simple, no schema, resets on restart (which is fine for a single operator who would re-plan on restart anyway since the on-disk files are the resume mechanism).
- **Optional Postgres persistence** later if the GUI needs historical job data.

This keeps things simple: the API service is a thin FastAPI wrapper around the existing synchronous functions, runs them in a background thread, and tracks state in memory. The `brief.yaml` / `take.yaml` files on disk are the actual state — the job tracker just knows where they are.

Let me present the plan clearly.

Here's what I found — full picture now, then questions.

**What exists**

- `scripts/campaign_api.py` is for the *show* player (`app/campaign/cli.py`), not the generator. Unrelated.
- The 3-layer generator (`utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/src/`) has **no HTTP layer at all** — it's a set of plain Python functions, called as scripts. There's no `main.py` yet (that was Part B we planned).
- Project HTTP stack is **FastAPI + uvicorn** exclusively. Every service (`message-api`, `control-panel`, `log-shipper`, `twitch-presence`) follows the same shape: own directory, own `requirements.txt`, own compose service name.
- No async-job/queue pattern anywhere in the repo yet. Generation runs are long (minutes to days) and threaded (`pool.py`), not asyncio-native.

**What the API needs to be**

A new FastAPI service, `services/3layer-generator/`, same shape as the others. The core generation code is already synchronous and file-based (brief.yaml / take.yaml on disk is the real state) — the API is a thin wrapper that drives those functions and reports progress.

5: bind-mounted read-only into every worker at /data/replays; they now live in

18: import, per-call connection, 5s connect timeout, autocommit): a worker

20: and get a clean available() == False rather than an ImportError at pane
21: startup.
22:

24: Degrading a failure into "no episodes" is the caller's job — that keeps
25: this module's failure modes visible to its tests, and lets each caller

69:         project = EXCLUDED.project,

71:         episode_date = EXCLUDED.episode_date,
72:         event_count = EXCLUDED.event_count,
73:         byte_size = EXCLUDED.byte_size,
74:         uploaded_by = EXCLUDED.uploaded_by,
75:         uploaded_at = now();
76: """
77: 
78: LOAD_SQL = "SELECT script FROM replay_episodes WHERE name = %(name)s;"
79: 
80: LIST_SQL = "SELECT name FROM replay_episodes ORDER BY name;"
81: 
82: LIST_DETAILED_SQL = """
83: SELECT name, project, session_id, episode_date, event_count, byte_size,
84:        uploaded_by, uploaded_at
85: FROM replay_episodes
86: ORDER BY name;
87: """
88: 
89: DELETE_SQL = "DELETE FROM replay_episodes WHERE name = %(name)s;"
90: 
91: 
92: def available():
93:     """True when this process can reach the store: psycopg2 importable and
94:     the POSTGRES_* env present. Same contract as narration_store.available()
95:     — callers treat False as "no library", never as an error."""
96:     if not all(os.environ.get(name) for name in _REQUIRED_ENV):
97:         return False
98:     try:
99:         import psycopg2  # noqa: F401
100:     except ImportError:
101:         return False
102:     return True
103: 
104: 
105: def _connect():
106:     import psycopg2
107:     conn = psycopg2.connect(
108:         host=os.environ.get("POSTGRES_HOST", "localhost"),
109:         port=os.environ.get("POSTGRES_PORT", "5432"),
110:         dbname=os.environ["POSTGRES_DB"],
111:         user=os.environ["POSTGRES_USER"],
112:         password=os.environ["POSTGRES_PASSWORD"],
113:         # A down DB must stall the pane for seconds, not minutes.
114:         connect_timeout=5,
115:     )
116:     conn.autocommit = True
117:     return conn
118: 
119: 
120: def ensure_schema():
121:     """Create the replay_episodes table if it doesn't exist. Raises on DB
122:     failure.
123: 
124:     Unlike messages/container_logs, no long-lived consumer owns this table,
125:     so there is no service whose startup would naturally create it —
126:     message-api calls this best-effort instead (docs/episode_store.md)."""
127:     conn = _connect()
128:     try:
129:         with conn.cursor() as cur:
130:             cur.execute(CREATE_TABLE_SQL)
131:     finally:
132:         conn.close()
133: 
134: 
135: def save_episode(name, script, uploaded_by="operator", overwrite=False):
136:     """Store one validated episode script. Returns True when the row was
137:     written, False when an episode of that name already existed and
138:     `overwrite` is False. Raises on DB failure.
139: 
140:     The caller is expected to have run episode_validator.validate_episode
141:     first — this module stores what it is given and does no redaction or
142:     schema checking of its own."""
143:     payload = json.dumps(script, ensure_ascii=False)
144:     params = {
145:         "name": name,
146:         "script": payload,
147:         "project": str(script.get("project") or ""),
148:         "session_id": str(script.get("session_id") or ""),
149:         "episode_date": str(script.get("date") or ""),
150:         "event_count": len(script.get("events") or []),
151:         "byte_size": len(payload.encode("utf-8")),
152:         "uploaded_by": uploaded_by,
153:     }
154:     conn = _connect()
155:     try:
156:         with conn.cursor() as cur:
157:             cur.execute(SAVE_OVERWRITE_SQL if overwrite else SAVE_SQL, params)
158:             # rowcount is 0 only when ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING suppressed it.
159:             return cur.rowcount > 0
160:     finally:
161:         conn.close()
162: 
163: 
164: def load_episode(name):
165:     """The episode script dict for `name`, or None when the library has no
166:     such episode. Raises on DB failure."""
167:     conn = _connect()
168:     try:
169:         with conn.cursor() as cur:
170:             cur.execute(LOAD_SQL, {"name": name})
171:             row = cur.fetchone()
172:     finally:
173:         conn.close()
174:     if not row:
175:         return None
176:     script = row[0]
177:     # psycopg2 decodes jsonb to dict already; tolerate a str for drivers or
178:     # test doubles that hand the raw column back.
179:     if isinstance(script, (str, bytes)):
180:         script = json.loads(script)
181:     return script
182: 
183: 
184: def list_episodes():
185:     """Sorted episode names — the pane's idle listing and the agent's random
186:     viewer_joined pick. Raises on DB failure."""
187:     conn = _connect()
188:     try:
189:         with conn.cursor() as cur:
190:             cur.execute(LIST_SQL)
191:             rows = cur.fetchall()
192:     finally:
193:         conn.close()
194:     return [row[0] for row in rows]
195: 
196: 
197: def list_episodes_detailed():
198:     """Every episode's metadata (no scripts) for message-api's GET /replays.
199:     Raises on DB failure."""
200:     conn = _connect()
201:     try:
202:         with conn.cursor() as cur:
203:             cur.execute(LIST_DETAILED_SQL)
204:             rows = cur.fetchall()
205:     finally:
206:         conn.close()
207:     return [{
208:         "name": row[0],
209:         "project": row[1],
210:         "session_id": row[2],
211:         "date": row[3],
212:         "event_count": row[4],
213:         "byte_size": row[5],
214:         "uploaded_by": row[6],
215:         "uploaded_at": row[7].isoformat() if row[7] is not None else None,
216:     } for row in rows]
217: 
218: 
219: def delete_episode(name):
220:     """Remove one episode. Returns True when a row was deleted, False when
221:     the name was already absent. Raises on DB failure."""
222:     conn = _connect()
223:     try:
224:         with conn.cursor() as cur:
225:             cur.execute(DELETE_SQL, {"name": name})
226:             return cur.rowcount > 0
227:     finally:
228:         conn.close()

## Assistant (Plan · qwen3.8:27b · 668.7s)

_Thinking:_

I'm working through the API design now, trying to figure out where the job runner should live. The generator code is in a utilities directory and runs against Ollama, so I need to pick a port for the new service (8092 seems reasonable) and decide on the architecture for executing jobs.

The key decision is whether the API directly calls generator functions in a background thread within the same process, or if it spawns a subprocess. In-memory execution is simpler since the generator code is already a set of functions, but I'd need to handle PYTHONPATH carefully and ensure the service has LLM connectivity to Ollama. The real constraint is that the service needs visibility into the same filesystem where briefs and takes are written—probably the shared volume used by the control panel and other services.

For the jobs table, I'm thinking it should live in the virtualtubers database and follow the same schema pattern as the other stores: id, pack, stage, status (queued/running/completed/failed/cancelled), config snapshot, progress fields, result summary, error message, and timestamps. I'd also need a helper module to manage available jobs, ensure the schema exists, and provide CRUD operations.

For long-running generation jobs, I'm planning to submit them and poll for progress rather than using WebSockets—the GUI can poll the jobs endpoint. I could support cancellation via a threading event that workers check, though that's a nice-to-have for phase two. The endpoints would be: submit a job with pack and stage details, list jobs filtered by pack, and get individual job status.

I'd also add a dry-run preview endpoint for the segment stage and a test-mode endpoint that runs one segment with a specified profile. The tricky part is that the generator code lives in `utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/src/` and uses relative imports, so I need to decide whether to COPY that whole directory into the service or keep it separate.

I'll go with COPYing the modules into the Docker image and setting PYTHONPATH to ensure imports work correctly, similar to how episode_store handles things. For the LLM, I need to pass the OLLAMA_URL environment variable and make sure the config's default base_url points to the right service — either the Ollama host on the LAN or the host.docker.internal address depending on the network setup.

For progress tracking during generation, I'm thinking of adding a callback hook to plan_segment and dialogue generation that reports on completed nodes or segments — this is new API-friendly surface but minimal. I'll keep the plan focused: a new job_store module and a jobs endpoint. For the jobs table schema, I'll add it to the mirrored SQL file if it exists, though schema changes are a small overhead.

The API layer stays read-only for now — the generator service handles writes to the output directory directly, and the GUI only reads and displays brief results. I'll skip GUI work since that's out of scope. For the port assignment, 8092 is available on the host side, though I need to verify the container's internal port mapping.

On the architecture side, I'm moving away from a pure CLI approach — the API becomes the primary interface instead. I'll keep the core logic functions intact, and for testing, a thin CLI script isn't strictly necessary but could be useful. Since the user wants an API, I'll structure the tests around the API layer using FastAPI's TestClient and mocks, which is cleaner than testing a CLI entry point. I'll document the final plan clearly for the user to approve, keeping it concise.

Grounded now. Here's the revised plan — same core build, but **Part B becomes an API service** and Part C plugs into it instead of a CLI.

## Final Build Plan (API version)

### Part A — Layer 2 recursive tree *(unchanged from before)*
`utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/`:
1. `config/generation.yaml` — `tree:` block replaces `chapters_per_segment`.
2. **Tests first** (decision 4): `tests/segment_helpers.py`, `tests/test_segment_schema.py`, `tests/test_plan_segment.py`.
3. `src/segment_schema.py` rewrite — prompts (`SYSTEM_PROMPT_EXPAND/LEAF`), `parse_children`, `leaf_eligible`, `derive_target_slots`, `distribute_words`, `validate_tree_config` (interlock), `validate_children`, `validate_slots(+node)` per-leaf density, `build_expand/leaf_prompt` (ancestor chain, M2 lore/spine content), `merge_brief` (tree→brief: `node_id` provenance, `nodes:`, `roll_points:`, `needs_rebrief:`).
4. `src/plan_segment.py` — continuous work queue on one `ThreadPoolExecutor`, branch task (retry→**forced-leaf degrade**), leaf task (retry→ERROR+empty), per-node checkpoint to `tree.yaml`+`brief.yaml`, segment-level sensitivity + word-count density checks (M4), tree-shape log.
5. qwen_worker: rewrite `specs/3layers_segment_schema.yaml` + `3layers_plan_segment.yaml`, run with `--model qwen3.8:27b`, review, promote (schema first, plan_segment second).
6. `pytest -q` green; Layer 1/worklist tests untouched as the brief-contract oracle.

### Part B — `services/3layer-generator/` (replaces the CLI plan)
New FastAPI service, same shape as `message-api` — `api.py`, `Dockerfile`, `requirements.txt`, compose entry.

1. **`job_store.py`** — follows `app/episode_store.py`'s pattern exactly (lazy psycopg2, per-call connection, `available()`, `ensure_schema()`, raises on DB failure):
   - Table `generation_jobs`: `id`, `pack`, `stage`, `profile`, `status` (queued/running/completed/failed/cancelled), `params` JSONB, `progress` JSONB, `result` JSONB, `error`, `submitted_by`, `created_at`, `started_at`, `finished_at`.
   - `submit / get / list / update_status / update_progress / cancel` (cancel = flag, generator checks between units).
2. **`runner.py`** — takes a job id, loads `config.yaml` + pack, resolves profile via `config.resolve_profile`, builds `PooledOllamaClient`, and calls the existing functions: `plan_arc` → `plan_segment` → `generate_segment_dialogue` (Part C). Progress callback writes to `job_store.update_progress`. Cancel event checked by the loop. Cancellation support is v1-simple (cooperative flag).
3. **`api.py`** endpoints:
   - `POST /jobs` — submit (`stage`, `pack`, `profile`, `segments`, `dry_run`, `test_profile`).
   - `GET /jobs` — list (filter by `pack`/`stage`/`status`).
   - `GET /jobs/{id}` — full record incl. progress + result summary.
   - `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel`
   - `POST /preview` — the `--dry-run` budget-arithmetic preview (no LLM call, no job row).
   - `GET /config` / `GET /config/tree` — read the resolved config for GUI display.
4. **`tests/test_3layer_api.py`** — `TestClient` + fake `job_store` (monkeypatch): submit→queued, running job returns progress, completion writes `result`, cancel flag honored, preview returns budget numbers with no LLM.
5. **Dockerfile** — `python:3.12-slim`, install `fastapi==0.141.1 starlette==1.6.0 uvicorn`, `httpx`, `pyyaml`, `psycopg2-binary`; COPY `app/llm_client.py`, `app/concurrent_llm.py`, `utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/src/*.py`, service files. `CMD ["uvicorn", "api:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]`.
6. **docker-compose.yml** entry — env: `POSTGRES_*` (job store), `OLLAMA_URL`, `PACKS_DIR` (bind to `campaigns/` or a shared path the workers use), `OUTPUT_DIR`. Port `8092:8000`. `depends_on: [postgres]` (if we add a real dep — see note below).

### Part C — Layer 3 `(generate_segment_dialogue.py)` — same as before
Local `generate_take`, `pool.run_pool` wiring, `dialogue.breaker:` config, hard neutral-take guard, spine-slot exclusion, `tests/test_generate_segment_dialogue.py`. **One change:** exposes a `progress_cb(units_done, units_total, stats)` parameter so both the CLI-less service and tests can observe progress without scraping logs.

### Part D — cross-cutting (reduced, as before)
Loki pushes for `plan_segment` / `generate_dialogue` / `generate_dialogue.failed` / `job.completed`, and the log-level pass.

### What I'm NOT doing
- No CLI `main.py` at all — the API is the interface.
- No GUI work (that's later, and it can `GET /jobs` + `POST /jobs` on 8092).
- No doc hygiene, no N1 benchmark, no events/forks/stingers.

### Two open items I need from you

1. **Shared filesystem**: the service reads `campaigns/<pack>/` and writes `output/<pack>/` — both live on the repo checkout. Compose already mounts `./` into the worker services. Do we bind-mount `./campaigns` and `./output` (or the full `./` as workers already do) into `3layer-generator`, or do you want a separate named volume?
2. **New table + no migration framework**: `docs/database_schema.md` references `docs/sql/01_create_tables.sql` etc. I'll add `generation_jobs` to the `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` in `job_store.py` (self-creating, like `replay_episodes`) — that's consistent with the repo's pattern. I'm *not* touching the `docs/sql/0*.sql` files unless you want me to, since there's no migration runner and those files would drift. OK?

## Final Build Plan

### Part A — Layer 2 recursive tree *(unchanged)*
In `utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/`:
1. `config/generation.yaml` — `tree:` block replaces `chapters_per_segment`.
2. Tests first (decision 4): `tests/segment_helpers.py`, `tests/test_segment_schema.py`, `tests/test_plan_segment.py`.
3. `src/segment_schema.py` rewrite — `SYSTEM_PROMPT_EXPAND/LEAF`, `parse_children`, `leaf_eligible`, `derive_target_slots`, `distribute_words`, `validate_tree_config`, `validate_children`, `validate_slots(+node)` per-leaf density, `build_expand/leaf_prompt` (ancestor chain, M2 lore/spine content), `merge_brief` (`node_id`, `nodes:`, `roll_points:`, `needs_rebrief:`).
4. `src/plan_segment.py` — continuous work queue, branch/leaf tasks (retry→forced-leaf degrade / ERROR+empty), per-node checkpoints, segment-level checks (sensitivity, slot-count, word-count density), tree-shape log. Add `progress_cb(done, total, node)` param so Part B can observe without scraping.
5. qwen_worker specs rewrite; run schema then plan_segment with `qwen3.8:27b`; review, promote.
6. `pytest -q` green; Layer 1 / worklist tests untouched as brief-contract oracle.

### Part B — `services/3layer-generator/` (API)
FastAPI service, same shape as `message-api`. **No Postgres — job state is file-based on the bind-mounted output dir.**

1. **`job_store.py`** — file-based job state:
   - Layout: `<output_root>/_jobs/<job_id>.json` (atomic write: temp + rename). One directory, no DB.
   - Shape: `{id, pack, stage, profile, status, params, progress, result, error, submitted_by, created_at, started_at, finished_at}`.
   - `submit / get / list / update_status / update_progress / cancel`. All mutations atomic via rename. Cancel sets a flag the runner polls between units.
2. **`runner.py`** — sequential dispatcher (thread, started at app boot):
   - Polls the job dir for `status=queued` (oldest submit first), sets `running`, executes:
     - `stage=arc` → `plan_arc`
     - `stage=segment` → `plan_segment` (with `progress_cb`)
     - `stage=dialogue` → Part C's `generate_segment_dialogue` (with `progress_cb`)
     - `stage=all` → arc → segment → dialogue in order
   - Writes `result` / `error` / `finished_at` and sets `completed`/`failed` on exit.
   - Cooperative cancel: polls `job_store.is_cancelled(id)` at each unit boundary.
3. **`api.py`** endpoints (all synchronous `def`, same style as `message-api`):
   - `POST /jobs` — body: `{pack, stage, profile?, segments?, dry_run?, test_profile?}`. Returns job with `status=queued`.
   - `GET /jobs?pack=&stage=&status=` — list (file listing, no scan of output content).
   - `GET /jobs/{id}`
   - `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel` — sets flag; 404 if not found, 409 if already terminal.
   - `POST /preview` — in-process dry-run, budget-arithmetic only, no LLM, no job row.
   - `GET /config?pack=` — loaded + profile-resolved config for GUI display.
   - `GET /healthz`
4. **`tests/test_3layer_api.py`** — `TestClient` + tmp-dir `job_store`:
   - submit→queued, list filters by pack/stage/status, get returns full record
   - cancel flag honored (fake runner that checks `is_cancelled` mid-run)
   - `POST /preview` returns numbers with zero LLM calls
   - unknown profile → 400 with message from `resolve_profile`
5. **Dockerfile** — `python:3.12-slim`, `fastapi==0.141.1`, `starlette==1.6.0`, `uvicorn`, `httpx`, `pyyaml`; `COPY app/llm_client.py`, `app/concurrent_llm.py`, `utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/src/*.py`, service files. `CMD ["uvicorn", "api:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000"]`.
6. **`docker-compose.yml`** entry:
   - `image: virtualtubers-3layer-generator:latest`, `pull_policy: never`
   - `environment:` `OUTPUT_DIR=/data/output`, `PACKS_DIR=/data/packs`, `OLLAMA_URL=${OLLAMA_URL:-http://host.docker.internal:11434}`, `GENERATOR_CONFIG=/data/config/generation.yaml`
   - `volumes:` `./campaigns:/data/packs:ro`, `./utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/output:/data/output` (bind mounts — data persists across restart, per your answer 1), `./utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/config:/data/config:ro`
   - `ports: "8092:8000"`, `restart: unless-stopped`. No `depends_on` needed (no stateful deps).

### Part C — Layer 3 (`src/generate_segment_dialogue.py`)
- `generate_take(improviser, unit, config)` — local, loop from brief, `conditions` via `carry`, `recent` reset, never raises.
- Wiring: shared `PooledOllamaClient`, `build_worklist` as resume, `estimate_seconds` log, `pool.run_pool` (per-thread improviser, writer = `_write_take` + `_append_manifest`), breaker from `dialogue.breaker:`.
- Config: `dialogue.breaker: {window: 50, failure_rate: 0.5, min_samples: 20}`.
- **Hard** neutral-take guard (non-zero return / exception) scanning disk after pool drain, spine-slot exclusion (test, not assumption).
- `progress_cb(units_done, units_total, stats)` param for the dispatcher.
- `tests/test_generate_segment_dialogue.py` — spine exclusion, isolation, loop reachability (issue #6), condition→prompt mapping, guard pass/fail, cancel mid-run.

### Part D — cross-cutting (reduced)
- Log-level pass on the new tree/pool code (TRACE in/out, DEBUG classification + LLM calls, INFO per-segment, ERROR in handlers).
- **Loki: deferred** (per your answer 2 — "sql logging can wait for later" and we've gone file-based, so no Loki wiring in this build; add later when the service stabilizes).

### Not doing
- No CLI `main.py`.
- No GUI work.
- No `docs/sql/` migration files.
- No N1 benchmark.
- No events / forks / stingers.
- No Loki pushes.

### GUI integration note (future, not now)
When the management GUI is built, it talks to `http://<host>:8092`:
- `POST /jobs` to kick off a generation
- `GET /jobs?stage=dialogue` for a status list
- `GET /jobs/{id}/progress-stream` — if the GUI needs live progress, I can add an `AsyncIterator` SSE endpoint in a follow-up (currently progress is a plain `GET /jobs/{id}` snapshot the GUI can poll)
- `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel` for "stop it"

# Coding Backend A/B Test — Build Plan (2026-07-03, overnight build)

## Goal

Let coder workers actually write code (not just narrate), via a **swappable
coding backend** selected per-worker in config, so three backends can be
compared head-to-head on identical tasks:

- `native` — our own minimal loop: existing `llm_client.py` + file-edit
  parsing + `git_client.py`
- `opencode` — shells out to `opencode run` (needs Node 18+ in image)
- `aider` — shells out to `aider --message` (isolated venv in image)

One new coder worker per backend (`coder-native`, `coder-opencode`,
`coder-aider`), each with its **own workspace volume** seeded from a sandbox
project, running the same `task_assignment` in parallel. Results logged to
Postgres for comparison.

## Locked decisions (user answered 2026-07-03)

1. **Git**: local commits only; push/PR built but no-op gracefully until
   `GIT_SERVER_URL` is set (user is standing up a local git server separately).
2. **Sandbox**: I create a small seeded-bug Python project as the agents'
   workspace. Everything logged.
3. **Image**: ONE `vtube-worker` image gains Node 18 + opencode + aider —
   backend choice is pure config.
4. **Tester**: real pytest execution replaces the weighted-random stub.

## Architecture decisions made during build

# 3LayersWeeklyGeneration — design decisions & next steps

Record of the 2026-08-18 planning session against
[utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/PLAN.md](../../utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/PLAN.md)
and the review backlog in [three_layers_generation_issues.md](three_layers_generation_issues.md).

Two sessions, both on 2026-08-18.

**Session A (D1-D9)** opened to resolve issues **#7** (takes semantics, 3x cost
swing) and **#2** (Layer 2 batching). Both resolved. It also turned up a larger
finding — **request concurrency**, not pipeline structure, is the dominant cost
lever — which changes the budget by ~5x and reshapes what Layers 2 and 3 have
to look like.

**Session B (D10-D18)** closed the last open design area: **efficiency,
random events, and story branching**. Its through-line is that branching is
affordable only if it is paid for in *state* rather than in *script* — see
D10's micro-drift vs macro-fork split, and D12, which buys the entire
random-event story for zero additional GPU time.

Measured baseline throughout, from `campaigns/ashiorid/generated/manifest.jsonl`
(24 takes): **105 words/take, 7.7 beats/take, 95.4 words/min single-stream**.

# 3LayersWeeklyGeneration API — Build Plan (2026-08-20)

## Goal

Turn the 3-layer offline content generator (`utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/`) from a script-only codebase into a **FastAPI service** that:

1. Implements the **recursive tree** for Layer 2 (decision D19, replacing per-segment flat chapters).
2. Implements Layer 3 (dialogue generation with the existing pool + worklist).
3. Exposes both as **async jobs** over HTTP, with **file-based job state** on the shared output dir (no Postgres dependency for the new service).
4. Will be plugged into the management GUI when it gets built (later, separately).

## Locked decisions (user answered this session)

1. **API, not CLI** — FastAPI service `services/3layer-generator/` following the same shape as `message-api` and `control-panel`.
2. **Job state = file-based** (on bind-mounted `./output/`) instead of a Postgres table. No new DB schema; no `PSQL` dependency. Job dir: `<output_root>/_jobs/<job_id>.json`, atomic writes (rename), all mutations serialized by a single dispatcher thread.
3. **Job execution = sequential, one at a time** in submission order, driven by a single dispatcher thread started at app boot. Rationale: the real bottleneck is the shared Ollama pool anyway; concurrency adds contention (progress file, circuit breaker window, neutral-take guard scope) for no measurable gain.
4. **No CLI `main.py`** — the API is the interface. The old CLI plan (Part B) is replaced.
5. **N1 concurrency benchmark** (build step 4) deferred to manual, between parts B and C. Not in this build.
6. **Doc hygiene deferred** — `PLAND.md` updates, D19 design decision entry, issues tracker update. Not in this build.
7. **Loki pushes deferred** — "log to a file for now." Not in this build. Add when the service stabilizes.
8. **Tests first** (per existing design decision 4) for the tree rewrite. Test file list below.
9. **Existing conventions**:
   - FastAPI pinned `fastapi==0.141.1` + `starlette==1.6.0` (pinned to match `message-api`).
   - Python 3.12-slim base image.
   - One service, one directory, one requirements file, one Dockerfile.
   - `psycopg2` NOT a dependency (no DB).
   - `pytest-timeout` already in repo requirements for the `pool.py` deadlock scenario.

## Scope

### In
- Tree model for Layer 2 (replaces flat `chapters_per_segment`).
- Continuous work queue for Layer 2 drain (no separate 2a/2b phases).
- Force-leaf degrade path (leaf prompt replaces expand prompt on expand exhaustion).
- Per-node checkpoint writes (`tree.yaml` + `brief.yaml`) between units.
- Segment-level checks (sensitivity budget on flattened slots, slot-count floor, word-count density floor).
- M2 content in prompts (ancestor chain, `target_words`, spine ground truth).
- M3 `roll_points:` list in brief.yaml.
- M5 `needs_rebrief:` flag on brief.yaml.
- M4 word-budget distribution (`distribute_words`, exact-sum).
- M1 parallelism invariant (branch tasks never depend on their own leaf tasks in the same drain pass; enforced by work-queue enqueue order).
- Layer 3 (`generate_segment_dialogue.py`) — full wiring: pool, worklist, breaker, writer, guards, cancel.
- API service: `POST /jobs`, `GET /jobs[?pack=&stage=&status=]`, `GET /jobs/{id}`, `POST /jobs/{id}/cancel`, `POST /preview`, `GET /config`, `GET /healthz`.
- File-based job store (atomic, dispatcher-serialized).
- Sequential dispatcher thread (submit → run in order).
- Progress callback plumbing (generator functions accept `progress_cb`).
- Cancellation (cooperative flag, polled between units).
- Test coverage per part below.
- Compose entry + Dockerfile + requirements.

### Out
- No GUI (separate, later build).
- No Postgres table or `docs/sql/` migration.
- No Loki pushes.
- No `PLAND.md` / D19 / issues-tracker doc updates.
- No `stingers` pass (needs `events.yaml`, not built).
- No events table, no forks, no TTS.
- No N1 benchmark in-build (manual between B and C).
- No `main.py` CLI.

## Architecture

### Layer

# 3LayersWeeklyGeneration — Build Plan (2026-08-20)

## Goal

Turn the 3-layer offline content generator from a test-suite of bare functions into an API-driven pipeline:

1. **Layer 2 rewrite** — replace "chapters" with a self-determining recursive node tree, sized by word-count thresholds, with forced-leaf degradation, continuous work-queue execution, and per-node resume.
2. **Layer 3** — the dialogue take generator on the existing `worklist` + `pool` harness.
3. **HTTP service** — `services/3layer-generator/` (FastAPI + uvicorn), the interface for the management GUI to come.

## Locked decisions

From the plan session (D10–D18 in `three_layers_design_decisions.md`), plus this session:

| # | Decision |
|---|----------|
| 1 | Word count is the *input* to the recursion; nodes split by `min_node_words`, leaf slots hit `derive_target_slots` — same ratio as before (~40 min / ~170 slots / 9 takes) |
| 2 | Recursive tree replaces the `chapters:` list; `brief.yaml` output **contract is unchanged** (slots list, `slot_id` uniqueness, `chapter_id`-equivalent provenance renamed to `node_id`) — worklist/Layer 1 tests stay the passing oracle |
| 3 | Node `words` is a **hint**; leaf prompts carry it, validation uses `leaf_density_floor` (default 0.8). Model gets target, not hard cap |
| 4 | **Tests first**: `tests/test_segment_schema.py` + `tests/test_plan_segment.py` written and failing, *then* `src/segment_schema.py` + `src/plan_segment.py` implemented — mirroring how `pool.py`/`worklist.py` were built |
| 5 | **Forced-leaf degradation**: after `max_attempts` failed expansions, the node becomes a leaf and gets slots for the *full node budget* (not the per-leaf share) — never a silent hole |
| 6 | Continuous work queue on ONE `ThreadPoolExecutor`: branch results enqueue children mid-drain. Parallelism precondition (M1, from D18): `concurrency ≥ 2 × expected_pending_branches` |
| 7 | **API instead of CLI** — new FastAPI service, same shape as `message-api` |
| 8 | **File-based job state** (not Postgres): `<output_root>/_jobs/<job_id>.json`, atomic temp+rename. Zero Postgres dependency for this service |
| 9 | **Sequential job execution** — one dispatcher thread, jobs in submission order. Ollama is the real bottleneck; parallel jobs would complicate shared pool, breaker, guard |
| 10 | **Bind mounts** for `campaigns/` (ro), `output/` (rw), `config/` (ro) so data survives container restarts |
| 11 | N1 concurrency benchmark: **manual**, run by you between B and C; not in this build |
| 12 | Doc hygiene (PLAND.md, D19, issues tracker): **deferred** to a follow-up |
| 13 | Loki pushes + SQL logging: **deferred** ("log to a file for now" — the job file *is* the log of record this build) |
| 14 | `--test-mode` from the flag list survives as `POST /jobs/{profile-test}` semantics — loop `profile_names(layer)` on one segment, output under `_test/<stage>/<profile>/` |
| 15 | `--stingers` → explicit rejection: "requires events.yaml, not yet built" |

## Deliverables

### Part A — `utilities/3LayersWeeklyGeneration/`

**A1. `config/generation.yaml`**
```yaml
# replaces segment.chapters_per_segment
segment:
  tree:
    max_leaf_slots: 250          # a node whose slots would exceed this MUST split (D14, M4)
    max_children: 5              # split fan-out cap (D14)
    max_depth: 2                 # absolute cap, only as a valve for degenerate packs (D14)
    min_node_words: 20000        # nodes at or below this become leaves (D14)
    leaf_density_floor: 0.8      # slots must sum to >= this * node words (D5)
```
Keep `targets:` as a *hint block, never authoritative* (D14, D17).

**A2. `src/segment_schema.py` (rewrite)**
- `SYSTEM_PROMPT_EXPAND`, `SYSTEM_PROMPT_LEAF` constants (replaces `_CHAPTERS`/`_SLOTS`)
- `parse_child_nodes(reply) -> list[dict]` — reuse existing fence-strip + YAML parse, key `nodes`
- `leaf_eligible(node, config) -> bool` — `words ≤ min_node_words` AND not past `max_depth`; `max_leaf_slots` and `max_children` are *enforced* at validation, not here
- `derive_target_slots(node_words) -> int` — `node_words / budget.target_words_per_hour / (words_per_take * takes_per_slot)`, rounded, min 1
- `allocate_child_words(parent_words, n_children) -> list[int]` — equal split to one decimal, residual to the last child so the sum is exactly the parent's
- `validate_tree_config(config) -> list[str]` — **raises** (not returns list, this is operator-error territory): `min_node_words ≥ 2 * derive_target_slots_max * words_per_take`, `max_depth ≥ 1`, `max_children ≥ 2`; cross-checks with `budget`
- `validate_children(children, config) -> list[str]` — each child `words ≥ min_node_words / max_children` (floor), non-empty `continuity_in`/`out`
- `validate_slots(slots, node, pack, vocab, config) -> list[str]` — existing checks + per-leaf density: `sum(len(prompt.split()) for s) ≥ leaf_density_floor * node.words`
- `build_expand_prompt(pack, node, config, problems) -> str` and `build_leaf_prompt(...)` — carry the **ancestor chain** (each ancestor's synopsis + continuity_in) so the model's choices at depth 3 aren't orphaned (D14); slot prompts must be **for the ear** like the scene narration (M2), legal lore stems shown from `pack.lore`, **explicit stop condition in target_words** (D7: the model must know exactly what "done" looks like)
- `check_sensitivity_budget(slots, config)` — unchanged, but now called **per segment** on the flattened slots, not per-chapter
- `merge_brief(nodes_tree, slots_by_node, config) -> dict` — walk the tree depth-first, emit **the same `brief.yaml` contract**: `slots:` list (each slot gains `node_id` instead of `chapter_id`), plus a `nodes:` summary (id, words, depth, leaf or not) so the GUI can render the tree

**A3. `src/plan_segment.py` (rewrite)**
- `plan_segment(pack, brief, config, llm, vocab, out_path, progress=None) -> dict` — signature adds `progress: Callable | None`; everything else unchanged so L1 tests and the CLI-less `main.py` still work
- Start: load `tree.yaml` if present (resume); else start with the **root node** for the segment (`words=segment_words, depth=0, no children`)
- Work queue (plain `queue.Queue` of node ids): branch nodes expand → children enqueued **into the same queue**; leaf nodes get slots directly. One `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=concurrency)` drains it. Per-node checkpoint: `tree.yaml` written atomically (temp+rename) after each node's children or slots land.
- Branch task: attempt loop (`max_attempts`), each attempt `parse_child_nodes` → `validate_tree_config`/`validate_children` → on success **distribute** `parent.words` to children via `allocate_child_words`, enqueue. On exhaust → **forced-leaf degrade** (decision 5): node becomes a leaf for its full budget, logged at WARN.
- Leaf task: attempt loop, `parse_leaf_slots` → `validate_slots(slots, node, pack, vocab)` → store. On exhaust → ERROR log, node left with empty slots (never fabricate), run continues.
- Finish: `check_sensitivity_budget(flattened_slots)` at segment level; `derive_target_slots` vs actual for **density check** (log WARN, don't fail); write `brief.yaml`; write `tree.yaml`; `progress(n_nodes, n_slots_done, n_units)` for the GUI
- Loki hooks: `{"app": "three_layers", "operation": "plan_segment"}` INFO at completion, ERROR in the degrade/fail paths

**A4. `tests/` (written FIRST, per D4)**
- `tests/segment_helpers.py` — factory for nodes, slots, briefs, `FakeLLM`
- `tests/test_segment_schema.py` — `allocate_child_words` sum-exactness at odd splits; `leaf_eligible` thresholds (at/below/above min, depth valve, max_leaf_slots); `validate_tree_config` interlock failures; `validate_children` floor; `validate_slots` per-leaf density floor; `build_expand/leaf_prompt` ancestor-chain rendering at depth 3; `check_sensitivity_budget` unchanged behavior; `parse_child_nodes` fence handling (reuse existing fixture shapes)
- `tests/test_plan_segment.py` — forced-leaf degrade after N failures; depth stop at `max_depth`; `concurrency ≥ 2×peaks` precondition (queue grows but doesn't starve under fake delays); resume from `tree.yaml`; `progress` callback firing counts

**A5. `tools/qwen_worker/specs/3layers_segment_schema.yaml` + `3layers_plan_segment.yaml`**— **rewrite** to match the new code, run under `--model qwen3.8:27b` for the acceptance pass, promote. The old spec is the spec from D10–D18's "chapters" design and is obsolete.

### Part B — `services/3layer-generator/` (new service)

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `services/3layer-generator/api.py` | FastAPI app + endpoints
